# Chart.js port differences

This file lists the hunks of ported code that do not translate the upstream lines
([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §4). Each hunk carries the line comment
`// DIVERGES(chartjs): <ID>, <reason>`. `check:headers` pairs each marker with a row of this file.
A port-only file is not a row. A hunk of a port-only file that gives a difference of a row carries
the marker of that row. An example is `jsGet` in `lib/src/js/js_object.dart`.

The ID starts with its category: `adapt`, `platform`, `theme`, `js-semantics`, `harness` or
`not-followed`. A skip reason `'diverges: <ID>'` names a row.

The column "Upstream file@version:lines" names the pin at which the porter last checked the row.
Each bump checks every row (the column "Re-check on bump"). It moves each Chart.js citation to the
new version, also when the file did not change, and it gives the lines at the new pin
([upstream-tracking.md](upstream-tracking.md) §4.9). Thus all Chart.js citations name one
version. The line citations in the column "Reason" are at the same pin. The commit pin
`v4.5.1+7169e651` (2026-10-20) moved all Chart.js citations from `v4.5.1`. Only the lines of
`helpers.dom.ts`, `element.arc.ts` and `index.d.ts` changed. The tag `v4.6.1` (2026-10-29) moved all Chart.js citations
from `v4.5.1+7169e651`. No cited file changed.

| ID | Dart file and symbol | Upstream file@version:lines | Category | Reason | Upstream reference | Pinning test | Re-check on bump |

| <a id="adapt-incremental-data-update"></a>`adapt-incremental-data-update` | `core.controller.dart` `updateData`; dataset/controller and native-scale opt-ins; plugin capabilities; port-only `core/data_update.dart` | No upstream data-only API; reuse of `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:475-540, 648-680 and `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:404-449, 701-708 | adapt | Explicit data-only lifecycle with fixed native ranges, static settings, native input/structure checks, metadata/stack refresh, compatible hooks, retained layout, and full fallback. With a kept layout, the `afterUpdate` of the legend skips `adjustHitBoxes`, because no `fit` ran. A callback of the event replay or of the draw may call `update`, as in a full update. | <a id="adapt-text-style-fonts"></a>`adapt-text-style-fonts` | port-only `lib/src/types/chart_text_style.dart` `ChartTextStyle`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.options.dart` `toFont`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.config.dart` `_mergeTextStyles`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `merger`, `mergerIf`; `lib/src/core/core.defaults.dart` the `font` default and the route read; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.canvas.dart` `renderText`, `longestText`, `LongestTextCache`; `lib/src/canvas/canvas.dart`, `canvas_ui.dart` and `canvas_ui_text.dart` `font`; the font reads of `core.scale.dart`, `scale.radialLinear.dart`, `scale.time.dart`, `plugin.legend.dart`, `plugin.title.dart`, `plugin.subtitle.dart` and `plugin.tooltip.dart`; `lib/src/types/index.dart` (no `FontSpec`, `CanvasFontSpec`); `lib/src/index.umd.dart`; `lib/src/theme/compile.dart`; `lib/src/typed/lower.dart` | `src/helpers/helpers.options.ts`@v4.6.1:7-8, 22-40, 115-141; `src/helpers/helpers.canvas.ts`@v4.6.1:25-31, 449; `src/helpers/helpers.config.ts`@v4.6.1:335-370; `src/core/core.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:54-60, 130-153; `src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.js`@v4.6.1:152-212; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:1812 | adapt | Upstream holds a font as a plain object `{family, size, style, weight, lineHeight}`, merges it key by key across the scopes, builds the CSS font text with `toFontString` and gives the text to the canvas, which parses it. In the port, a font is a Flutter `TextStyle` from the option to the canvas, also in a raw config ([Chart.js ADR 0116](adr/0116-text-style-fonts.md)). `ChartTextStyle` extends `TextStyle` with the field `lineHeight`, `fontSize * height`, which its constructor computes once. `merge` and `copyWith` give a new `ChartTextStyle`. The resolver merges the `TextStyle`s of the scopes that upstream gathers for a font object (`_mergeTextStyles`), a route and `helpers.merge` merge two fonts with `TextStyle.merge`, and `toFont` merges a font over `defaults.font` or its fallback. The layout reads `lineHeight` and `fontSize`. The canvas lays out each text with the whole `TextStyle`: a letter spacing of an app also changes the width. These upstream values have no form: a line height in pixels, in `em` or in percent, a size as text, the style `oblique` (an app gives `FontStyle.italic`), the weights `lighter` and `bolder`, and the style keywords `initial`, `inherit` and `unset`. A family list is `fontFamily` and `fontFamilyFallback`. `toFontString`, `toLineHeight`, the style check of `toFont` and its warning, the CSS parse of the canvas and the kind `FontSpec` of the schema are gone. A weight from 501 to 549 gives the bold face of the test font, as the CSS rule of `@font-face` fonts does. Chrome with system fonts gives the regular face. In the tests, the generators write a font object of a config as a `ChartTextStyle` literal (`dartFont` of `tool/reference/font_record.mjs`) and a font text of the canvas as a record of its fields (`fontRecord`). | — | `test/specs/helpers.options_test.dart`; `test/port/helpers/helpers.options_test.dart`; `test/port/helpers/helpers.config_test.dart` and `test/port/core/core.defaults_test.dart`, groups `adapt-text-style-fonts`; `test/port/canvas/canvas_ui_text_test.dart`; the font records of the reference tests | Compare `toFont`, `toFontString`, `createSubResolver`, the route of `core.defaults.js` and each font read of the plugins and the scales on each bump. |

| <a id="adapt-radial-line-fan"></a>`adapt-radial-line-fan` | `lib/src/scales/scale.radialLinear.dart` `_drawStaticAngleLines`; optional `canvas_line_fan.dart`; native `canvas_ui.dart` `tryStrokeLineFan` | `src/scales/scale.radialLinear.js`@v4.6.1:592-618 | adapt | Under the static plan of `adapt-radial-static-plans`, the angle lines after the first one are offered to a guarded native operation: one `Canvas.drawRawPoints(PointMode.lines, ...)` in place of `beginPath`, `moveTo`, `lineTo` and `stroke` for each line. No path is built. The engine draws each line of the call as its own stroke, in order, so a translucent line blends over the lines before it. The bytes are equal on `flutter_tester` on Skia on the CPU, Impeller on Vulkan and Impeller on Metal for each color, width, cap, composite operation, clip and similarity transform; the signed distance route of the macOS embedder is equal by its source. GPU Skia, the web, a dash, a gradient or pattern, another transform than an invertible similarity, an unnotified or disposed canvas, a line with no length and a coordinate that is not finite or above 1e30 keep the ordinary strokes, from the index after the first line. **Observable difference:** none in pixels, results, hooks or callbacks on the engines above. The first read that can run the build runs it with these options and keeps the items until the next mark. The guards run no user code and return at the first failed test: the built-in generator of the exact controller of dataset 0 at index 0 first, then only the keys of that generator, a cached `default` element option entry, falsy `filter` and `sort`, plain `reverse`, native chart, config, data, `labels` and `datasets`, and at a mark `display` exactly `false`. A failed guard at a mark builds at once, as upstream. A read runs the guards again, adds no meta and reads no new options, so no read and no guard runs user code or throws, and a read before the next mark gives the snapshot of upstream after an option change. The differences: a read after an in-place change with no update gives the new values; a read in a hook after the dataset update gives the style of this update; a read that cannot run the build gives the last built items, which can be older than the snapshot of upstream, from an update before the last one. That read is one after dataset 0 became a new object (a write at index 0, a new `datasets` List, the `data` setter, a new `ChartView.raw` config, a new key of a typed dataset, an insert at index 0) or another dataset (a removal, a new order); after `data.labels` became no native List or an empty one, or `data.datasets` an empty one; in the dataset update before its `default` entry exists (`beforeDatasetUpdate`, and until `afterUpdate` for a polar area update in another mode and for colors that became indexed or scriptable in this update); from a box that is no longer `chart.legend`; or from a box after a `stop` with failed guards. The build still waits after such a read, until a `stop`. A polar area update in another mode runs `getStyle(0)`, the first call of the ordinary build, when an entry of another mode and no cache clear prove that it calls no option. A hook that adds a function value under a new key of `defaults.elements.arc` after that dataset update makes this `getStyle(0)` call the function once more, in that update. Apart from this, hook order, callback order and count and pixels do not change. Native indexed Lists leave no cached entry: a mark also waits when the exact chart, Config, arcs and native scopes hold a scalar or a native indexed List of native entries on each key of the arc element scopes, for the indexes of the labels. An ordinary build writes the context of its first resolve before it reads the next keys of the arcs and `filter`, `sort` and `reverse`, so this guard first needs native scopes behind the legend and label resolvers, a native arc 0, `display` exactly `false`, falsy `filter` and `sort` and plain `reverse`, and it takes each of these keys and each key of the arcs by a peek with no write to the cache and no sub resolver. An object, a function or a custom class leaves each read at its own point of the ordinary build, and a visible legend never takes this guard. It keeps the plan and the count of the arcs of this mark for a read, which needs no new resolver: a read in a next update before its layout mark gives what the last `afterUpdate` built, also after the update rebuilt the arcs. The mark writes the state of each skipped `getStyle(i)` with a scalar loop: `getContext(i, false, 'default')` in the order of the items, also on an active arc, with the ordinary call for a new context and past the arcs. A read writes no context. The differences of an indexed mark: a read after an in-place change of a List with no update gives the new entries, while each entry that an index of a label reaches is still native for its key. A read gives the last built items, which can be older than the snapshot of upstream, from before the last update, and the build still waits, after any of these in-place changes with no update: such an entry is no longer native for its key (an object or a function on any key; a scalar of another type than the slot of the key, such as a String or `null` on a numeric key like `borderWidth`, or a number on `borderAlign`), a List of a varying key became empty, or item 0 of the raw `borderDash` List became an object. The same holds after a write through the resolver. All else is as above. | <a id="adapt-range-updates"></a>`adapt-range-updates` | `core.controller.dart` `Chart.updateRanges`, `_canUpdateRanges`, `_updateRanges`, `_updateScaleRanges`, `_recordUpdateState`; `core.datasetController.dart` `configureKeptResolver`; `core.plugins.dart` `PluginService.supportsRangeUpdates`, `refreshDescriptorViews`; `helpers.config.dart` `OptionView.resolver`; `core.config.dart` `debugCacheSizes` (tests only); `plugin.colors.dart` `rangeUpdateGuard`; port-only `core/data_update.dart` `RangeUpdateSnapshot`, `types/plugin.dart` `Plugin.rangeUpdateGuard`, `types/index.dart` `Chart.updateRanges` | No upstream range-only API; reuse of `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:316-380, :415-455, :475-549, `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:438-445 and `src/core/core.plugins.js`@v4.6.1:73-83, :175-188 | adapt | An explicit opt-in for a change of the `min` and `max` options of the scales only, written or deleted through a view of the options. The configuration of the last full update stays: no `config.update()` (no cache clear, no scale merge), no new resolver, no id writes and no axis, type and position checks of the scales, no `buildOrUpdateControllers`, `_checkEventBindings` or `_updateHiddenIndices`, no new plugin descriptors. Each place where `update` makes a new resolver gets a new context view over the kept one: the chart options, each plugin descriptor (with the descriptor defaults of `pluginOpts`) and each dataset (`configure` keeps the parsing read and the clears of the two caches). `scale.init` runs for each scale with the scale options of the new chart view, in the key order of `options.scales` as in `buildOrUpdateScales`, and the boxes of the scales go and come back as in `update`. The visibility of each meta is written as in `buildOrUpdateControllers`. Each hook, the parse, the layout, the dataset updates, the replay and the draw run as in `update`, and each scriptable option that these steps read runs again; the options that only the skipped steps read (`responsive`, the chart `indexAxis`, the `axis` and `position` of a scale) keep the results of the last full update. Checks that send the call to `update(mode)`: a nested call, no range state since the last invalidation (the points of the data-only state of Chart.js ADR 0074; a cancelled or thrown update), a chart or config of another class, a plugin that does not opt in or whose `rangeUpdateGuard` fails (the colors plugin with `forceOverride`, which writes new color Lists into the datasets in each layout), a notified data change, another `data` or `options` object, another dataset, another chart type or another inline plugin List (its identity and length), a controller of another class. The caller keeps the contract: no other change in place since the last full update, also no plugin registered or unregistered. A range update ends as a full update ends (the tokens of Chart.js ADR 0074). | <a id="adapt-intl-number-format"></a>`adapt-intl-number-format` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.intl.dart` `_getNumberFormat`, `formatNumber`, `formatNumberDigits`, `localeOf`, `numberFormatOf`; `lib/src/core/core.ticks.dart` `_numeric`; `lib/src/core/scale_options.dart` `TickOptions.format` | `src/helpers/helpers.intl.ts`@v4.6.1:2-17; `src/core/core.ticks.js`@v4.6.1:33, 56-59; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:1702, 3380 | adapt | Upstream formats with `new Intl.NumberFormat(locale, options)`: the chart option `locale` is a BCP 47 tag, and the tick option `format` is an object of `Intl.NumberFormatOptions` that `formatters.numeric` puts over its computed `{notation, minimumFractionDigits, maximumFractionDigits}`. The port formats with the `NumberFormat` of package:intl as it is ([Chart.js ADR 0119](adr/0119-plain-intl-number-format.md)). The option `locale` holds a Flutter `Locale`, and `ticks.format` holds a `NumberFormat`, also in a raw config: a language tag as text or an options object stops a debug build, and a release build takes no locale or no format. A `NumberFormat` formats a tick as it is: Dart cannot copy it, so the computed digits do not go under it. Without a format, the decimal or the scientific pattern of the locale with the digits of the tick spacing formats the tick. A `Locale` without intl data, and no locale, give the current intl locale (`Intl.defaultLocale`); the resolved locale is in the cache key. The cache key is the record of the intl locale, the notation and the digits, not `locale + JSON.stringify(options)`. The strings are the package:intl strings with its defects, which the port does not correct: for example `10E14` for `1e15`, wrong digits above 2^63, and wrong digits for more than 15 fraction digits on the web ([dart-lang/i18n#440](https://github.com/dart-lang/i18n/issues/440)). The reference generators write a `Locale` and a `NumberFormat`; a case gives both digit keys of a format, so that upstream and the port agree. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.intl_test.dart`; `test/port/core/core.ticks_test.dart`, group `formatters.numeric` | Compare `helpers.intl.ts` and `formatters.numeric` on each bump. |

| <a id="adapt-rounded-rect-path"></a>`adapt-rounded-rect-path` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.canvas.dart` `addRoundedRectPath`; the `roundRect` members that it calls: `lib/src/canvas/canvas.dart` `CanvasPath.roundRect`, `Path2D.roundRect`, `roundRectGeometry`; `lib/src/canvas/canvas_ui_path.dart` `UiPathBuilder.roundRect`; `lib/src/canvas/canvas_ui.dart` `fill`, `clip`; `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/src/context.dart` `MockContext.roundRect`; the caller `lib/src/plugins/plugin.legend.dart` `Legend._draw` (the box of an item with a border radius, `plugin.legend.js:346-353`); the caller `lib/src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.dart` `Tooltip._drawColorBox` (the color box of a label color with a border radius, `plugin.tooltip.js:832-855`) | `src/helpers/helpers.canvas.ts`@v4.6.1:498-527 | adapt | Upstream adds four arcs and four lines. The port makes one call, `ctx.roundRect(x, y, w, h, [topLeft, topRight, bottomRight, bottomLeft])`: the HTML `roundRect`, with the flips of Chromium and Firefox for a negative width or height. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1). Under an axis-aligned transform, the `dart:ui` canvas adds one rounded rectangle to the path. It fills it with `drawRRect`, clips with `clipRRect`, and fills the border ring of a bar (`element.bar.js:188-192`) with `drawDRRect`. When the inner rectangle of the ring has no width or no height, it fills the outer shape alone with `drawRect` or `drawRRect`. The figures are in [performance.md](performance.md) §8.4 and §13.6. The fill and clip area is the same. These differences are observable: (1) the path is a closed subpath and then a new subpath at (x, y). Thus a stroke has a join and no caps at the start point (x + topLeft, y), and the next path call starts at (x, y). (2) When the radii of one side add up to more than its length, all radii scale down by one factor. Upstream arcs overlap there. (3) A current point is not joined by a line to the rounded rectangle. (4) A non-finite x, y, w, h or radius adds nothing. Upstream adds the parts of the path with finite points. (5) A negative radius throws a `RangeError` before the call adds a part. Upstream throws an `IndexSizeError` from `arc` after it added the parts before that corner. Both forms throw. (6) A negative width or height gives the flipped rectangle. Upstream gives lines that cross. (7) The pattern of a dashed stroke starts at another point: `ui.Path.addRRect` starts its contour on the left edge and goes clockwise, and upstream starts at (x + topLeft, y) and goes counterclockwise. This is display-only ([Chart.js ADR 0034](adr/0034-no-display-only-js-exactness.md)). The mock context records one `roundRect` call. The tests that compare calls with an upstream run translate it back into the upstream arcs and lines (`test/port/helpers/rounded_rect_upstream.dart`). | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.canvas_test.dart`, group `addRoundedRectPath`; `test/port/helpers/helpers.canvas_ui_test.dart`, group `adapt-rounded-rect-path`; `test/port/canvas/canvas_ui_round_rect_test.dart`; `test/port/elements/element.bar_test.dart`, group `draw`; `test/port/elements/element.bar_ui_test.dart`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.legend_test.dart`, the reference case `useBorderRadius`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.tooltip_test.dart`, the reference case `a label color with a border radius` | On each bump, compare `addRoundedRectPath` and its callers (`element.bar.js`, `plugin.legend.js`, `plugin.tooltip.js`, `scale.radialLinear.js`). If upstream calls `ctx.roundRect`, port that call and remove this row. |
| <a id="adapt-spline-control-point-nan"></a>`adapt-spline-control-point-nan` | `lib/src/types/geometric.dart` `SplinePoint` (`cp1x`, `cp1y`, `cp2x`, `cp2y`); `lib/src/helpers/helpers.curve.dart` `_capBezierPoints`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.interpolation.dart` `bezierInterpolation` | `src/types/geometric.d.ts`@v4.6.1:48-51; `src/helpers/helpers.curve.ts`@v4.6.1:173-179, 212-215; `src/helpers/helpers.interpolation.ts`@v4.6.1:33-34 | adapt | Upstream leaves an unset control point `undefined`. Thus a write does not box the value. `ToNumber` of `undefined` is NaN, and both values are falsy. Only `=== undefined`, `in`, `Object.keys`, `typeof` and `JSON.stringify` tell them apart. No upstream `src` file applies these to a control point (grep at v4.5.1). A computed NaN and an unset control point now look the same. They already look the same to every upstream reader. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.curve_test.dart`, group `adapt-spline-control-point-nan`; `test/port/helpers/helpers.interpolation_test.dart` test `bezierInterpolation without control points gives NaN` | Compare the control-point readers on each bump: `element.line.js`, `helpers.canvas.ts` `_bezierCurveTo`, `plugin.filler` and `controller.radar`. |
| <a id="adapt-log10-powers"></a>`adapt-log10-powers` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.math.dart` `log10` | `src/helpers/helpers.math.ts`@v4.6.1:18 | adapt | Upstream `log10` is `Math.log10`. Dart has no `log10`, and `log(x) / ln10` gives `2.9999999999999996` for 1000, where the floor of a logarithm decides the tick spacing, the decimals of the labels and the logarithmic ticks. The port computes `log(x) * log10e` and gives the exponent of an exact power of ten ([Chart.js ADR 0122](adr/0122-dart-math-without-fdlibm.md)), as the fallback of earlier Chart.js versions did. Between the powers of ten, the last bit can differ from V8. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.math_test.dart`, group `log10` | Compare `helpers.math.ts` on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-dart-math"></a>`js-semantics-dart-math` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.easing.dart` `_elasticIn`, `_elasticOut`, `effects` (`easeInSine`, `easeOutSine`, `easeInOutSine`, `easeInExpo`, `easeOutExpo`, `easeInOutExpo`); `lib/src/kurkle_color/srgb.dart` `_to`, `_from`; `test/specs/helpers.easing_test.dart` `_toEqual` | `src/helpers/helpers.easing.ts`@v4.6.1:4-5, 47-59; `test/specs/helpers.easing.tests.js`@v4.6.1:41-57; @kurkle/color `src/srgb.js`@v0.3.2:7-8 | js-semantics | Upstream calls `Math.sin`, `Math.cos`, `Math.atan2`, `Math.log` and `Math.pow`. The port calls `dart:math` `sin`, `cos`, `atan2`, `log` and `pow` with double operands at each site ([Chart.js ADR 0122](adr/0122-dart-math-without-fdlibm.md)). The last bit of a result can differ from V8 and between the VM, dart2js, dart2wasm and the operating systems. At a site whose result reaches a decision, for example the hit test of an arc through `getAngleFromPoint`, the difference changes the result only for an input exactly on a boundary: a point exactly on the border of two arcs. The controller reference tests list such points (`hitBorders`). The upstream spec rounds each result to 1e-8. Some expected values lie on a rounding tie, for example `easeInElastic(0.2)`. Thus the spec allows one unit of 1e-8. The sign of a zero stays exact. | — | `test/specs/helpers.easing_test.dart`; `test/port/kurkle_color/index.esm_test.dart`, group `srgb.js` (the `interpolate` bytes of `_to` and `_from` match V8) | Compare the `Math.sin`, `Math.cos` and `Math.pow` calls of `helpers.easing.ts` and `srgb.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-strict-this"></a>`js-semantics-strict-this` | `test/specs/helpers.core_test.dart` `callback` case `should call fn with the given scope` | `test/specs/helpers.core.tests.js`@v4.6.1:142-155 | js-semantics | The upstream spy is a sloppy-mode function. JavaScript gives it the global object `window` as `this` when `thisArg` is missing, `null` or `undefined`. Dart has no global object. A `JsFunction` gets `this` as the caller gives it, as a strict-mode function does (`lib/src/js/js_function.dart`). Thus the case expects `undefined`, `null` and `undefined` in place of `window`. The expect of the given scope is the upstream expect. `callback` passes `thisArg` unchanged, as `fn.apply(thisArg, args)` does. | — | `test/specs/helpers.core_test.dart` case `should call fn with the given scope`; `test/port/js/js_function_test.dart` test `gets null and undefined as this, as a strict-mode function` | Compare the `this` expects of `helpers.core.tests.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-to-upper-case"></a>`js-semantics-to-upper-case` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `capitalize` | `src/helpers/helpers.core.ts`@v4.6.1:386-388 | js-semantics | Upstream calls the JavaScript `toUpperCase`. It applies the full Unicode case mappings of the engine. The port calls the Dart `String.toUpperCase` of the platform. On the Dart VM, it applies only one-to-one mappings from an older Unicode table. Over the BMP, 306 code units give another result than the reference engine, for example `'ß'` (`'SS'` in JavaScript) and `'ꭰ'`. dart2js and dart2wasm call the `toUpperCase` of the JavaScript engine. Callers capitalize the names of options and methods (`helpers.config.ts:214`, `core.registry.js:152`, `helpers.collection.ts:131`). ASCII names give the same result on every platform. A JavaScript-exact table of 1,270 code units changes only non-ASCII option names (Chart.js ADR 0034). | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.core_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-to-upper-case` | Compare `_capitalize` and its callers on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-set-equality"></a>`js-semantics-set-equality` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `setsEqual` | `src/helpers/helpers.core.ts`@v4.6.1:396-408 | js-semantics | A Dart `Set` stands for a JavaScript `Set`. `Set.prototype.has` compares with SameValueZero. The port honors the supplied Dart Set equality policy. The two agree on strings, the element type of the upstream callers (`core.controller.js:559`, `:597`), and on `0` and `-0`. A set using `==` does not find NaN; default backend sets can differ (Flutter JavaScript/DDC finds it). Tests supply an explicit comparator to pin the policy independently of the backend. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.core_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-set-equality` | Compare the element types of the `setsEqual` callers on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-resolver-cache"></a>`js-semantics-resolver-cache` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `resolveObjectKey`, `_keyResolvers` | `src/helpers/helpers.core.ts`@v4.6.1:336-342, 378-381 | js-semantics | Upstream keeps its resolver cache in an object literal. Thus a key that names a member of `Object.prototype` finds that member and not a resolver. In the reference engine, `resolveObjectKey({constructor: 5}, 'constructor')` returns the object, `'toString'` returns `'[object Undefined]'`, and `'__proto__'`, `'hasOwnProperty'` and `'valueOf'` throw a `TypeError`. The port keeps the cache in a `Map`, which has no prototype (Chart.js ADR 0043). These keys resolve as any other key. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.core_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-resolver-cache` | Compare `keyResolvers` and `resolveObjectKey` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-merge-element-target"></a>`adapt-merge-element-target` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `merge`, `merger`, `mergeIf`, `_getProp`, `_setProp`, `_hasOwn` | `src/helpers/helpers.core.ts`@v4.6.1:53-55, 190-209, 220-233, 260-286, 301-304 | adapt | Upstream `isObject` is true for an instance of a JavaScript class, also for an element. Thus `merge` writes into an element target (`plugin.tooltip.tests.js:1501`, `:1527`, `:1553`, `:1579`). The port `isObject` is true only for a `Map<String, Object?>` and a typed object with a face (`JsObjectFace`, [Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §5). `merge` writes into a `types.PropertyAccess` target with the read and the write by name, and `merger` merges an object into a value of that type. `merge` returns the target. Thus `merge(tooltip, view)` gives the upstream writes. Five differences stay. `clone` keeps an element as a reference, where upstream copies its own keys into an object without a prototype. `mergeIf` returns an element target and writes nothing, where upstream writes the keys that the element does not own: `mergerIf` tests the own keys of its target, and an element has no list of its own keys. `merge` skips an element source, where upstream copies its own keys. An element source value replaces the target value, also a Map or an element, where upstream merges the own keys of the element into a Map or an element target value (`merge({a: {k: 1}}, {a: element})` keeps `k` upstream). Such a target has no list of own keys: `jsObjectKeys` gives no key for it, and the own-key test of `mergerIf` throws an `UnsupportedError`. A custom `merger` gets the target as an `Object`. No Chart.js merger lists the keys of its target, and no module of `src/` merges into a class instance or from a class instance. The spec result does not depend on the deep merge: `Object.assign` gives the same 37 draw calls in the four text align cases. | [Chart.js ADR 0039](adr/0039-tooltip-model-and-animation.md) §10 | `test/port/helpers/helpers.core_test.dart`, group `adapt-merge-element-target` | Compare `isObject`, `clone`, `_merger`, `merge`, `mergeIf` and `_mergerIf`, and search `src/` for a `merge` or `mergeIf` with a class instance target or source. |
| <a id="adapt-typed-scope-merge"></a>`adapt-typed-scope-merge` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.core.dart` `merge`, `_mergeScope`, `_cloneForKey`, `_cloneToScope`; `lib/src/options/option_scope.dart` (port-only) `typedMergeKeys`, `typedMergeTarget`, `normalizeKey`, `ownNormalized`, `lookupNormalized`, `classifyNormalized`, `storeNormalized`, `debugTypedMerge` | `src/helpers/helpers.core.ts`@v4.6.1:190-209, 211-213, 220-233, 260-286, 301-322 | adapt | Upstream `merge` takes `Object.keys(source)` and calls the merger with each key, which reads `target[key]` and `source[key]`, tests `hasOwnProperty` (`_mergerIf`) and writes `target[key] = clone(sval)`. On typed scopes ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §3, §5) each of these steps went through the face by name: a key list, a lookup of the name for each read, test and write, and the copy of a nested object under a key of a nested kind (`_cloneToScope`) the same. When the merger is the default `merger` or `mergerIf`, the target is a typed scope with the face of its class (`OptionScope.nativeFace`) and no route, and a source is a typed scope with the face of its class and no extra whose name is an array index, `merge` walks the source by its typed keys (`_mergeScope`): the own keys in the `Object.keys` order (the key snapshot of Chart.js ADR 0081), the reserved names skipped, the own cell of the target, the cell that `jsRead` reads from the source (`lookup`, a route included), the same recursion for two objects, and for a write the `clone` copy, or the typed copy of the nested kind, stored with the cell of `jsWrite` (the classification of the key, the audit of a foreign value of the oracle O2, the throw of a frozen target). `_cloneToScope` copies such a source by its typed keys in the same way. A Map, a scope of a subclass, a routed target, a source with an array-index extra, a custom merger and every other object keep the walk by name. The keys, their order, the values, the cells, the kinds of the nested scopes, the new objects, the audit calls and the throws are the ones of the walk by name; only the lookups of the names and the key lists go. The change is for speed only (zoom profiles of 2026-11-10: `mergeScaleConfig` was 7.7-11.2% of a 1000-point input step). | — | `test/port/options/typed_merge_test.dart`, group `adapt-typed-scope-merge` (each case runs both walks through `OptionScope.debugTypedMerge`); `test/port/options/observation_test.dart` (O1) | Compare `clone`, `_merger`, `merge`, `mergeIf` and `_mergerIf` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-array-events"></a>`platform-array-events` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.collection.dart` `listenArrayEvents`, `notifyArrayListeners`, `unlistenArrayEvents`. `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `DatasetController._dataCheck`; `lib/src/js/js_array.dart` `jsSetIndex` | `src/helpers/helpers.collection.ts`@v4.6.1:130-149, 174-176; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:386-388 | platform | Upstream redefines `push`, `pop`, `shift`, `splice` and `unshift` on the data array with `Object.defineProperty`. Each new method calls the original method and then the `_onData*` member of each listener. A Dart `List` has no methods that a program can redefine. Thus `listenArrayEvents` keeps the listeners in the `_chartjs` stub (an `Expando`) and hooks no method. `unlistenArrayEvents` has no method to delete. The port-only `notifyArrayListeners` holds the body of the hooked method after the original call. The code that alters the array calls it. Each listener gets the items with the element type of its `listenArrayEvents` call. An `int` argument is a JavaScript number: the listener gets a `double`. Upstream also notifies `splice(start)` and `splice()`. Then `_onDataSplice` reads `arguments.length - 2` as a negative count (`core.datasetController.js:1064-1071`). `onDataSplice` has no form for that count. Thus a `'splice'` notification with fewer than two arguments throws an `ArgumentError`. The caller passes the `deleteCount` that the change used. `onDataSplice` passes the start of the notification unchanged, as upstream does. A start past the length of the elements throws a `RangeError` at the insert of the elements: in the notification with `parsing: false`, and in the next `buildOrUpdateElements` with parsing. Upstream leaves holes in the element array there, and its next `_update` throws a `TypeError`. The dataset controller listens to its data `List`. It gets the `_onData*` calls through `notifyArrayListeners`. A change of the `List` without that call notifies no listener. `_resyncElements` (`core.datasetController.js:962-989`) then parses the data again and adds or removes elements at the end. Thus the chart shows the new data, but an insert or a removal at the start or in the middle does not animate as upstream does. Dart has no `Object.isExtensible` for a `List`. Thus `_dataCheck` listens to each data `List`. Upstream skips a frozen, sealed or non-extensible array. A string `data` gets no listener, as upstream (`core.datasetController.js:386`): `_dataCheck` keeps it as a `JsStringList`. A `List` that cannot grow, for example a `List.unmodifiable`, gets no `push`, `splice` or `unshift`. `parse` writes the parsed items with `jsSetIndex` (`core.datasetController.js:461-463`, Chart.js ADR 0036): a write at the length appends, and a write past the length throws a `RangeError`. `jsSetIndex` also throws a `RangeError` for a negative index, where JavaScript writes the property `'-1'`. No caller gives a negative index: a negative splice start fails before the write, at the insert of the elements. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.collection_test.dart`, group `platform-array-events`. `test/port/core/core.datasetController_test.dart`, groups `platform-array-events` and `string data`. `test/specs/core.datasetController_test.dart`, the case of `core.datasetController.tests.js:841`: the effect of the listener on the elements stands for the `_chartjs` stub and the hooked methods | Compare `listenArrayEvents`, `unlistenArrayEvents`, `_dataCheck` and the `_onData*` members of `core.datasetController.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-request-anim-frame"></a>`platform-request-anim-frame` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.extras.dart` `requestAnimFrame` | `src/helpers/helpers.extras.ts`@v4.6.1:14-21 | platform | Upstream uses `window.requestAnimationFrame`. Without a `window`, it calls the callback at once. The port calls `SchedulerBinding.instance.scheduleFrameCallback` and returns the callback id (Chart.js ADR 0016, decision 1). The callback ignores the frame time stamp. Two differences are observable. A direct call of `requestAnimFrame` without a Flutter binding throws, where upstream calls the callback at once. The upstream callers pass `window` as the receiver (`core.animator.js:44`, `helpers.extras.ts:39`). Thus only a direct call of the exported helper reaches the upstream fallback. The microtasks of a frame run after all the frame callbacks of that frame. A browser runs them after each `requestAnimationFrame` callback. The promise jobs of the animator callback run at its checkpoint (row [`platform-animation-promise-checkpoint`](#platform-animation-promise-checkpoint), [Chart.js ADR 0019](adr/0019-animation.md) §7). Other microtasks, also the promise jobs that a throttled callback queues, run after all frame callbacks of the frame. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.extras_test.dart`, group `platform-request-anim-frame` | Compare `requestAnimFrame` and its callers on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-debounce-args"></a>`js-semantics-debounce-args` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.extras.dart` `debounce` | `src/helpers/helpers.extras.ts`@v4.6.1:55 | js-semantics | Upstream calls `setTimeout(fn, delay, args)` in the delayed branch. Thus `fn` gets the argument array as one argument. The immediate branch calls `fn.apply(this, args)` and spreads the array. The port gives `fn` the latest argument value in both branches (Chart.js ADR 0016, decision 3). Upstream gives `chart.update` the mode `['resize']` after a delayed resize, and `'resize'` or `'attach'` after an immediate resize (`core.controller.js:173`, `:283`). Three consumers see the difference: `controller.radar.js:62` tests `mode !== 'resize'`; the plugin hooks `beforeUpdate`, `afterUpdate`, `beforeDatasetsUpdate`, `afterDatasetsUpdate`, `beforeDatasetUpdate` and `afterDatasetUpdate` get `mode` (`core.controller.js:490`, `:524`, `:649`, `:661`, `:671`); the scriptable and animation option contexts get `context.mode` (`core.datasetController.js:780`). The transition keys do not change, because `` `transitions.${['resize']}` `` is `transitions.resize`. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.extras_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-debounce-args`. The chart-level test is `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-debounce-args`: after a delayed resize, `beforeUpdate` and `afterUpdate` get the mode `'resize'`. | Compare `debounce` and the `mode` readers on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-debounce-cancel"></a>`platform-debounce-cancel` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.extras.dart` `debounce`, `Debounced` | `src/helpers/helpers.extras.ts`@v4.6.1:50-61 | platform | Upstream returns a plain function and never clears its timeout. The port returns a `Debounced<T>`. Its `call(T)` method is the upstream function. Its port-only `cancel()` method cancels the pending `Timer` (Chart.js ADR 0016, decision 4). `Chart.destroy()` calls `_doResize.cancel()` (row `platform-destroy-cancel-resize`, phase 5). flutter_test fails a test with a pending `Timer` after the tree unmounts. Flutter widgets cancel their timers in `dispose`. Upstream lets the timer call `update()` on a destroyed chart. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.extras_test.dart`, group `platform-debounce-cancel` | Compare `debounce` and the `destroy()` of `core.controller.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-destroy-cancel-resize"></a>`platform-destroy-cancel-resize` | `lib/src/core/core.controller.dart` `Chart.destroy` | `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:937-955 | platform | Upstream `destroy()` leaves the pending timeout of `_doResize`. With a `resizeDelay`, that timer then calls `update()` on the destroyed chart. The port calls `_doResize.cancel()` after `_stop()` ([Chart.js ADR 0016](adr/0016-frame-scheduling.md), decision 4). Thus no update reaches a destroyed chart, and no `Timer` stays pending after the widget tree unmounts. flutter_test fails a test with a pending `Timer`. Only `destroy()` cancels: a detach keeps the pending update, as upstream. | — | `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `platform-destroy-cancel-resize`: with `resizeDelay: 50`, a resize and then `destroy()` give no `update` after `tester.pump(const Duration(milliseconds: 60))`, and the test ends with no pending `Timer` | Compare `destroy()` and the `_doResize` calls of `core.controller.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-animation-number-path"></a>`adapt-animation-number-path` | `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `Animation.tick`, `_tickNumber`, `_writeNumber`, the field `_element`, `_retarget`; `lib/src/core/core.element.dart` `Element.writeNumber` and its overrides in `lib/src/elements/element.bar.dart` and `element.arc.dart` and its calls in the constructor, `update` and the setters of `from` and `to` | `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:28-45, 51-65, 76-103 | adapt | Upstream ticks each animation with its interpolator `this._fn` and writes the value by name (`:102`). In the port, an animation with the built-in `number` interpolator and two `double` values ticks on two `double` fields ([Chart.js ADR 0019](adr/0019-animation.md) §6). `_retarget` selects the path. The constructor, `update` and the setters of `from` and `to` call it. A write to an `Element` goes through `Element.writeNumber` ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §10): the setters of `x` and `y`, and for an element of a built-in class the setters of its own numbers (`base`, `width` and `height` of a bar, the angles and radii of an arc). Another property, and a number of an element of a user subclass, is the write by name. A setter stores the state of the write by name. The writes, their order, their values and their types are the upstream ones. The `undefined` state, JS `null`, strings and booleans take the generic path, the upstream lines. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1). At 20000 points with two properties, the path takes 0.28 to 0.37 ms off each frame on wasm (probe J1 of Chart.js ADR 0019 §11). In the port, one animator frame of 20000 points with two properties takes 503 to 518 us on AOT. [performance.md](performance.md) §10.1 has the figures of the probe and of the port. | — | `test/port/core/core.animation_test.dart`, group `adapt-animation-number-path` | Compare `tick`, `update` and `interpolators.number` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-animation-color-cache"></a>`adapt-animation-color-cache` | `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `_ColorInterpolator`, the `Animation` constructor | `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:15-21, 35 | adapt | Upstream parses `from` and `to` with `helpers.color` in each call of the color interpolator (`:16-17`). In the port, each color animation gets its own interpolator object ([Chart.js ADR 0019](adr/0019-animation.md) §5). It keeps the parsed colors of its last call. It compares the next values with `identical`, and a new value gives a new parse. It mixes a clone of the cached target color, because `mix` changes its receiver. A `from` or a `to` that is not a String takes the upstream lines. The results are the upstream results. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1). A color tick takes 437 to 489 ns in place of 1832 to 1943 ns on AOT. In the port, one animator frame of 1000 Maps with two colors takes 556 to 570 us on AOT. [performance.md](performance.md) §10.1 has the figures. | — | `test/port/core/core.animation_test.dart`, group `adapt-animation-color-cache` | Compare `interpolators.color`, `helpers.color` and `mix` of @kurkle/color on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-color-animation-by-value"></a>`adapt-color-animation-by-value` | `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `_interpolatorOf` | `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:6-25, 35 | adapt | Upstream selects the interpolator of an animation with `interpolators[cfg.type || typeof from]`. A color is a string there, and `typeof` gives no interpolator for a string. Thus an animation of a color needs `type: 'color'`, and without it the first tick throws a `TypeError`. In the port, a color is a `dart:ui` `Color` ([Chart.js ADR 0061](adr/0061-native-flutter-colors.md)). `_interpolatorOf` also gives the color interpolator for a `Color` value. Thus the animation of one color property needs no `type`: an item `BorderColorAnimation`, `BackgroundColorAnimation` or `ColorAnimation` of `ChartConfig.animations` (row `adapt-property-animations`) names no type. An animation with `type: 'color'` and each animation of a number or a boolean do not change. A value of another type still has no interpolator. | — | `test/port/core/core.animation_test.dart`, group `adapt-color-animation-by-value`; `test/port/typed/animations_test.dart` | Compare `interpolators` and the read of `cfg.type` in `core.animation.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-property-animations"></a>`adapt-property-animations` | `lib/src/options/property_animation.dart` `AnimationSettings`, `PropertyAnimation` and its 19 classes, `ChartTransition` and its 5 classes, `ChartAnimationItems`, `transitionIn`, `transitionModeOf`; `lib/src/options/animated_prop.dart` `AnimatedProp.itemIn`, `_AnimatedBy`; `lib/src/core/core.controller.dart` the `Chart` constructor, `propertyAnimations`, `chartTransitions`; `lib/src/core/core.animations.dart` the `Animations` constructor, `_setItems`, `itemOf`, `_animates`, `_createAnimations`, `_createTypedAnimations`; `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `Animation.typed`, `Animation.update`, `_cfgDuration`, `_cfgDelay`, `_cfgEasing`, `_cfgLoop`, `animationHasDuration`; `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `resolveAnimations`; `lib/src/typed/groups.dart` `lowerAnimation`, `lowerTransitions`; `lib/src/widgets/chart_view.dart` `_appConfig` | `src/core/core.animations.js`@v4.6.1:7-41, 68-116; `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:28-45, 51-65; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:841-865 | adapt | Upstream sets the animation of one property with an entry of `options.animations` by the name of the property. `Animations.configure` copies the options of each entry into its table. The typed API has one ready class for each of the 19 animated properties of the elements (`RadiusAnimation`, `BorderColorAnimation`, ...), with the parameters `duration`, `delay`, `easing` (a Flutter `Curve`) and `loop`, and the constructor `off` (the duration 0). `ChartConfig.animations` is a List of these items, with no name of a property and no key. An item is not written into the options. `ChartView` gives the Lists to the chart: they are the fields `propertyAnimations` and `chartTransitions` of `Chart`, also parameters of its constructor, because the first update runs there. `DatasetController.resolveAnimations` gives them to `Animations`, and `Animation` reads the parameters of the item itself: a parameter that is not `null` goes before the value of the options of the property. A property with an item and no options of its own takes the `animation` options of the resolver, as an entry of `animations` does through `_fallback`. A change of a property animates when the duration of its item is not 0. Two items of one class stop a debug build. The items apply to the elements of the datasets, not to the tooltip, which has its own `animations`. The `ChartAnimation` of `ChartConfig.animation` goes the same way for `duration`, `delay`, `easing` and `loop`: the scope `options.animation` holds no copy of these parameters. Each of these cells holds the `ChartAnimation` itself (`lowerAnimation` of `lib/src/typed/groups.dart`), so the cascade of the options keeps the upstream order (a transition mode goes before the chart animation), and `_cfgDuration`, `_cfgDelay`, `_cfgEasing` and `_cfgLoop` of `core.animation.dart` read the parameter from the object (`AnimationSettings`). A read of `options.animation.duration` of a typed config gives that object, not a number. `ChartConfig.transitions` is a List with one ready class for each update mode (`HoverTransition` for `active`, `ShowTransition`, `HideTransition`, `ResizeTransition`, `ResetTransition`). The four parameters of an item go as those of the `ChartAnimation`: the cells of `options.transitions.<mode>.animation` hold the item itself, so a parameter of the mode goes before the chart animation, as upstream. The items of `ChartTransition.animations` go to `Animations` as the items of the chart do, and go before them in that mode. `PropertyAnimation` and `ChartTransition` are abstract classes with the base `AnimationSettings`. The type of an item names its property or its mode, and an item has no member with a name. A built-in `AnimatedProp` has its animation class as a type argument, and `AnimatedProp.itemIn` finds the item of the property by the type of the item, with no table. `transitionModeOf` gives the update mode of a transition class, because an update mode is still a name in `Chart.update`. Another library can add a class: the chart leaves it, and that library finds its items by type. A chart with no item, and each raw config, takes the upstream path. | — | `test/port/typed/animations_test.dart` | Compare `Animations.configure`, `_createAnimations` and the constructor and `update` of `Animation` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-flutter-curves"></a>`adapt-flutter-curves` | `lib/src/options/opt.dart` `classifyTyped<Curve>`; `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `Animation._easing`, `_cfgEasing`, `_ease`; `lib/src/core/core.animations.defaults.dart` `applyAnimationsDefaults`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.dart` the defaults of `tooltip`; `lib/src/theme/compile.dart` `_transitions`; the hand fixtures of `test/fixtures/hand/` with an `easing` | `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:1, 36, 100; `src/core/core.animations.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:8, 66; `src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.js`@v4.6.1:1312, 1320; `src/helpers/helpers.easing.ts`@v4.6.1:12-122 | adapt | Upstream keeps `easing` as a name and reads the function `effects[cfg.easing] || effects.linear` when it makes an animation (`core.animation.js:36`). In the port, the value of `easing` is a Flutter `Curve`, also in a raw config: the typed model holds the curve from the config to the animation, and the animation calls `Curve.transform`. The library has no easing names and no table from a name to a curve. The defaults are `Curves.easeOutQuart` and `Curves.linear`. An easing name is a value of the wrong type: its option cell is foreign (`classifyTyped<Curve>`), a debug build stops on it when it makes the animation (`_cfgEasing`), and a release build takes `Curves.linear`, the upstream result for a value that is not a key of `effects`. A read of `options.animation.easing` gives a `Curve`. A new entry of `easingEffects` has no effect on an animation: an app or a plugin gives its own `Curve` class, with no registration. `Curves` draws 24 of the 31 upstream names (quad, cubic, quart, quint, sine, expo, circ and back) as cubic Bezier curves. They differ from the formulas of `helpers.easing.ts` by 2% to 19% of the range in the middle of an animation, and one call takes about 26 ns in place of 5 ns (a probe of 1,001 points on the VM, 2026-11-11). The start and the end are the same. The linear, elastic (`ElasticInCurve(0.3)`, `ElasticOutCurve(0.3)`, `ElasticInOutCurve(0.45)`) and bounce curves give the upstream values. The maintainer accepted the difference of the display (2026-11-11). `_ease` keeps NaN for the NaN factor of a loop with the duration 0, because `Curve.transform` takes only the range 0 to 1. Upstream data hold an easing name in that place. No test compares the value of `easing` with upstream data: both sides get a marker (`withCurveMarkers` of `test/support/easing_json.dart`). For an upstream config, the test harness gives a curve with the formula of the upstream effect (`EffectCurve` and `nativeFixtureEasing` of `chartjs_test_utils`), so the animation reference tests keep the upstream steps. No code maps a name to a constant of `Curves`. `helpers.easing.dart` stays the port of the helper `easingEffects`, with its spec; no animation reads it. | — | `test/port/options/curve_option_test.dart` | Compare the three reads of `easing` in `core.animation.js` on each bump. |

An enum is the complete type: the scale code closes each set, and a scale of an app or a plugin with another rule reads its own option. A keyword is a value of the wrong type: its option cell is foreign (`classifyTyped`), and a debug build stops on it at its read (`ScaleBounds.of` and the others in the facades of `scale_options.dart`). The typed API has `bounds` on the five cartesian axes, and `align`, `crossAlign` and `source` on `AxisTicks`. The title `align` and the legend `align` (the place of a box) are other options and stay. In the tests, the fixture runner gives the enum value for a keyword of an upstream JSON fixture by the name of the value and by the key of the enclosing object (`_scaleEnumValues` of `native_colors.dart`, no table of names); the generators write the enum value (`SCALE_ENUMS` of `tool/reference/scale_chart_common.mjs` and of `scale_reference.mjs`; the second ran again and gave the bytes of the scripted change); a compare with upstream JSON text prints the name of the value (`keywordJson` of `test/support/keyword_enum_json.dart`). | — | `test/port/core/scale_options_test.dart`; `test/port/scales/`; `test/port/typed/lowering_test.dart` | Compare the reads of the four options on each bump. |

The name of each value is the keyword. The layout code closes the set, so an enum is the complete type. A keyword is a value of the wrong type: its option cell is foreign (`classifyTyped`), and a debug build stops on it at its read (`ChartAlign.of`). In the port, the union `LayoutPosition` is an interface: the enum `ChartPosition` gives the names, and the class `ScaleValuePosition(scaleId, value)` the object form, also in a raw config. The name of each value is the keyword. The layout closes the set of names. The legend and a scale take a `LayoutPosition`, the title and the subtitle a `ChartPosition`. In the port, `cutout` and `radius` hold a `RadiusLength` and `grace` a `GraceLength`, also in a raw config: each form computes itself. `PercentRadius(N)` is `'N%'` and `PixelRadius(n)` the number; `PercentGrace(N)` is `'N%'` and `ValueGrace(n)` the number. The base classes are open: a new form, from a bump or from a plugin, is a subclass, and the readers do not change. The doughnut reads `cutout.fractionOf(maxSize)` and `radius.pixelsOf(maxRadius)`; `addGrace` takes a `GraceLength` and reads `grace.amountOf((max - min) / 2)`. The math of each form is the upstream math of its value. A value that is not a length gives the result of the unary `+` for JS `null` (0) and NaN for another value; a text stops a debug build. `toPercentage` and `toDimension` are not in `lib/helpers.dart` or in the UMD `Chart.helpers`. The defaults are `PercentRadius(50)` (doughnut `cutout`), `PixelRadius(0)` (pie `cutout`), `PercentRadius(100)` (`radius`) and `ValueGrace(0)` (`grace`). The typed `ChartLength` is gone: the typed API takes a `RadiusLength`. In the tests, the generators write the length classes (`keyedValue` of `tool/reference/scale_chart_common.mjs`: `cutout` and `grace` by key, `radius` for a percent text only, because a number `radius` is also the radius of a point), a compare with upstream JSON text puts a marker on both sides (`withLengthMarkers` of `test/support/length_json.dart`), and the 8 data fixtures with a length are hand ports ([Chart.js ADR 0115](adr/0115-hand-ports-of-typed-data-fixtures.md)). | — | `test/specs/controller.doughnut_test.dart`; `test/port/helpers/helpers.options_test.dart`, group `_addGrace`; `test/port/controllers/arc_options_test.dart`; `test/port/typed/lowering_test.dart` | Compare `toPercentage`, `toDimension` and their callers on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-axis-objects"></a>`adapt-axis-objects` | `lib/src/types/chart_axis.dart` `ScaleAxis`, `CartesianAxis`, `XAxis`, `YAxis`, `RAxis`; `lib/src/core/chart_axis_reads.dart` `sameAxis`, `axisDataKey`; `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `getIndexAxis`, `datasetIndexAxis`, `determineAxis`, `_getAxisFromDefaultScaleID`, `_getDefaultScaleIDFromAxis`, `_idMatchesAxis`, `_axisFromPosition`, `_getAxisFromDataset`, `_mergeScaleConfig`; `lib/src/core/core.controller.dart` `buildOrUpdateScales`, `buildOrUpdateControllers`; `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `linkScales`, `parsedValueOf`, `axisStackOf`, `setPointAxis`; `lib/src/options/parsed.dart` `ParsedItem`, `ItemStacks`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.collection.dart` `lookupByAxis`, `rlookupByAxis`; the axis reads of the controllers, `core.scale.dart`, `core.interaction.dart`, `core.layouts.dart`, `helpers.curve.dart`, `helpers.extras.dart`, `plugin.decimation.dart` and `plugin.filler/`; the `indexAxis` defaults of `controller.doughnut.dart`, `controller.polarArea.dart`, `controller.radar.dart` and `core.defaults.dart`; `lib/src/typed/config.dart` and `lib/src/typed/datasets.dart` `indexAxis` | `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:5-53, 95-101; `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:331-337, 432; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:304-311; `src/controllers/controller.doughnut.js`@v4.6.1:77; `src/controllers/controller.polarArea.js`@v4.6.1:23; `src/controllers/controller.radar.js`@v4.6.1:14; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:71, 482, 1317 | adapt | Upstream holds an axis as a letter: the `indexAxis` of a chart, a dataset and a controller (`'x'`, `'y'`, `'r'`), and the `axis` of a scale. The core compares the letters, and it reads a parsed item, a stack object and the properties of a point by the letter. In the port, an axis is a class with no members: `XAxis`, `YAxis` and `RAxis`. `CartesianAxis` holds only `XAxis` and `YAxis`. The `indexAxis` of a chart and of a cartesian dataset, also in the typed API, has this type, so `RAxis` cannot go there. The `indexAxis` defaults of doughnut, pie, polar area and radar hold `RAxis`. `ScaleAxis` holds any axis: the `axis` of a scale, `Scale.axis` and `meta.indexAxis`. The core reads an axis with pattern matching, also in a raw config. `getIndexAxis` takes the `RAxis` of the defaults of a radial type first, and `datasetIndexAxis` does the same before the `indexAxis` of a dataset. Thus a cartesian axis of the options cannot reach a radial chart, where upstream takes it and throws a `TypeError` later. A value that is not an axis class is no axis, and a letter stops a debug build. A scale id `x`, `y` or `r`, or an id that starts with such a letter, still gives its axis, as upstream: a scale id is a name of the user. `sameAxis` compares two axes. `axisDataKey` gives the key of an axis in a plain object of the user: a data item of `parsing: false`, a `_stacks` object, a property of a point. An axis of another class has no field and no key: a read gives `undefined`, and no write occurs. `AxisKey` is gone: `ParsedItem` and `ItemStacks` take the axis class. `lookupByAxis` and `rlookupByAxis` search a table by an axis class; `lookupByKey` and `rlookupByKey` keep the search by a name. The enum `IndexAxis` of the typed API is gone. In the tests, the generators write the axis classes (`keyedValue` of `tool/reference/scale_chart_common.mjs` and the own writers), and the 35 data fixtures with an axis letter are hand ports ([Chart.js ADR 0115](adr/0115-hand-ports-of-typed-data-fixtures.md)). | — | `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, groups `getIndexAxis` and `determineAxis`; `test/port/options/parsed_test.dart`; `test/port/core/core.datasetController_plumbing_test.dart`; `test/port/typed/config_test.dart`, group `indexAxis` | Compare the axis reads of `core.config.js`, `core.controller.js` and `core.datasetController.js`, and the `indexAxis` defaults of the controllers, on each bump. |

The native option plans count an enum value as a scalar. The typed API has no `borderSkipped` field: an app gives the enum value through `extra`. The unit `'isoWeek'` of upstream `startOf(time, 'isoWeek', weekday)` is not a unit of the scale: it is the member `startOfIsoWeek(time, weekday)`. The default adapter reads no unit name: the aliases of moment (`'ms'`, `'days'`, `'W'`) and the exchange of a numeric unit with the amount of moment `add` do not exist. A value that is not a unit gives the results of moment for an unknown unit: the time for `startOf` and `endOf`, the difference in ms for `diff`, no change for `add`. In the scale, an option value that is not a unit is the automatic unit, as a falsy value is upstream; a name stops a debug build (`TimeUnit.of`). A `minUnit` that is not a unit throws the `TypeError` of upstream. The display format of a unit reads the key of the scope with the name of the unit. The typed API keeps `TimeAxis(unit: TimeUnit.month)`; a custom unit name of the old name type is gone.
