| <a id="adapt-animation-spec"></a>`adapt-animation-spec` | `lib/src/core/animation_spec.dart` `AnimationSpec`; `lib/src/core/core.animations.dart` `Animations.configure` | `src/core/core.animations.js`@v4.6.1:26-29 | adapt | Upstream copies the animation options of each config entry into a plain object (`:26`). The port makes an `AnimationSpec` ([Chart.js ADR 0019](adr/0019-animation.md) §4). It is a Map with a field for each key of `defaults.animation` and a Map of extra keys. It gives the Map results of a plain object with the same entries. Only the key order can differ. The keys come in the fixed order of `AnimationSpec.knownKeys`, then the extra keys. Upstream writes them in the `Object.keys` order of `defaults.animation` (:18, :26-29). The order differs when `defaults.animation` does not hold its known keys in the order of `knownKeys`: for example after a known key is deleted and added again, after a program replaces `defaults.animation` with `{duration: 500, delay: 0}`, or after a known key is written after an extra key. No Chart.js code reads the keys of this object. `configure` reads each option of a resolver with its `OptionKey` (`readOption`, [Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §10) and writes it with `AnimationSpec.storeKey`. The constructor of `Animation` reads the values with field reads. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1). In the port, one constructor takes 185 to 189 ns with an `AnimationSpec` in place of 298 to 300 ns with a Map config on AOT. An update of 20000 points makes 40000 animations. [performance.md](performance.md) §10.1 has the figures. | — | `test/port/core/animation_spec_test.dart`; `test/port/core/core.animation_test.dart`, group `adapt-animation-spec` | Compare `configure` and the keys of `defaults.animation` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-animation-promise-checkpoint"></a>`platform-animation-promise-checkpoint` | `lib/src/core/core.animation.dart` `Animation.wait`, `Animation._notify`; `lib/src/core/core.animator.dart` `Animator._refreshMethod`; `lib/src/js/js_promise.dart` `jsRunWithMicrotaskCheckpoint` | `src/core/core.animation.js`@v4.6.1:105-118; `src/core/core.animator.js`@v4.6.1:44-51 | platform | A browser runs the promise jobs after each `requestAnimationFrame` callback. Flutter runs the microtasks of a frame after all its frame callbacks ([Chart.js ADR 0016](adr/0016-frame-scheduling.md)). The port runs the animator callback inside `jsRunWithMicrotaskCheckpoint` ([Chart.js ADR 0019](adr/0019-animation.md) §7). `wait` returns the future of a `Completer.sync()`, and `_notify` settles it in a job of `jsEnqueuePromiseJob`. Thus a promise chain that the animator callback starts finishes at the end of that callback, as in a browser. The reason is the resolved option. Without the checkpoint, a re-hover in the frame that ends an un-hover animates a copy of the options. The microtask after the frame then gives the element the shared options. The element then keeps the color without hover under the pointer. One difference stays. The jobs that a throttled event callback queues run after all the frame callbacks of the frame, not at the end of that callback. Inside one checkpoint, a continuation runs in the job that settles its future. `Promise.all` settles in the reaction job of its last item, and JavaScript runs its `then` in a later job. `jsPromiseAll` gives the same order: it settles its result in a later job of `jsEnqueuePromiseJob`. `_animateOptions` reads that order. When the progress listener of the frame that ends a `$shared` options chain starts an update with an empty `awaitAll`, the target keeps the options of the earlier chain, as upstream. | — | `test/port/core/core.animator_test.dart`, group `platform-animation-promise-checkpoint`; `test/port/js/js_promise_test.dart` | Compare `wait`, `_notify`, `awaitAll`, `_animateOptions` and `_refresh` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-reduced-motion"></a>`platform-reduced-motion` | `lib/src/core/core.animations.dart` `Animations._createAnimations`; `lib/src/core/reduced_motion.dart` `isReducedMotion` | `src/core/core.animations.js`@v4.6.1:68, 88-103 | platform | Upstream has no reduced-motion code. For a canvas with the flag of `ChartView` (`MediaQuery.maybeDisableAnimationsOf`), `_createAnimations` reads no animation config: each value goes to its target at once (:99-103), and a running animation of the property is cancelled (:94-97). `onComplete` runs once for each render, and `onProgress` does not run. The resolved options do not change. Only `ChartView` sets the flag; no spec sets it ([Chart.js ADR 0049](adr/0049-chart-view-widget.md) §7). The read is one `Expando` lookup in each call. [performance.md](performance.md) §15.3 has the figures of the night series A-N3: no interval lies wholly above 0. | — | `test/port/core/core.animations_test.dart`, group `platform-reduced-motion`; `test/port/widgets/chart_view_test.dart`, group `reduced motion` | Compare `_createAnimations` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-animator-mute"></a>`platform-animator-mute` | `lib/src/core/core.animator.dart` `Animator.mute`, `Animator.unmute`, `Animator._muted` | `src/core/core.animator.js`@v4.6.1:12-18, 135-210 | platform | Upstream has no mute. The chart host follows `TickerMode` ([Chart.js ADR 0016](adr/0016-frame-scheduling.md) decision 7). It owns no chart ([Chart.js ADR 0037](adr/0037-legend-and-tooltip-input-path.md) §2), so it reports a change through `ChartHost.onTickerModeChanged`. The owner of the chart calls `mute` when `TickerMode` turns off, and `unmute` when `TickerMode` turns on. It also mutes a chart that it creates under `TickerMode(enabled: false)`. `mute` marks the chart in an `Expando`. Thus a destroyed chart leaves no entry. `unmute` removes the mark. If the chart has animations, it starts the loop again. `unmute` reads the record of the chart and creates no record. Thus the unmute of a destroyed chart keeps no entry either. The animations then continue at the current `Date.now()`, as a muted `Ticker` lets the time pass. The reason: a covered route with `maintainState` keeps its chart in the tree with `TickerMode` off. Without the mute, a looping animation there gives the app a new frame at each vsync. | — | `test/port/core/core.animator_test.dart`, group `platform-animator-mute`. The chart-level test of Chart.js ADR 0016 decision 11 is `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `platform-animator-mute`: a chart with `animation.loop: true` under `TickerMode(enabled: false)` schedules no frame, and it continues when `TickerMode` turns on. The owner of the chart connects `ChartHost.onTickerModeChanged` to `mute` and `unmute`. | Compare the API of `Animator` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-animator-skip-muted"></a>`platform-animator-skip-muted` | `lib/src/core/core.animator.dart` `Animator.update` | `src/core/core.animator.js`@v4.6.1:57-107 | platform | `_update` skips a muted chart, as it skips a chart that does not run ([Chart.js ADR 0016](adr/0016-frame-scheduling.md) decision 7). A muted chart does not count in `remaining`. When all charts with animations are muted, the loop ends, and the animator schedules no more frames. While a chart is muted, its `onComplete` and `onProgress` callbacks wait. A `render()` call on a muted chart with animations starts the loop again. Then the loop ends after one frame. | — | `test/port/core/core.animator_test.dart`, group `platform-animator-skip-muted` | Compare `_update` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-resolver-cached-read"></a>`adapt-resolver-cached-read` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.config.dart` `OptionResolver._get`, `OptionView._get` | `src/helpers/helpers.config.ts`@v4.6.1:64-67, 150-153, 218-230 | adapt | Upstream calls `_cached` with a new arrow function for each property read. `_cached` tests `hasOwnProperty`, then reads the value. The two `get` traps of the port have `_cached` inline: they read the own value of the cache object one time. An owned key gives its value, also `null` and `undefined`, as `_cached` does. Else the trap resolves the key with `_resolveWithPrefixes` or `_resolveWithContext`, writes the value into the cache object and returns it, as the arrow function and `_cached` do. The results are the same, and each key resolves one time. The typed reads `readKey` of Chart.js ADR 0054 §4 (row `adapt-typed-options`) read the cache in the same way. The change is for speed only: a cached read makes one lookup and no closure. On the Map resolver of Chart.js ADR 0043, the bench case `resolver/warm_read10` (10 cached reads) went from about 250 ns to 100 ns on the VM (AOT), from 550 ns to 210 ns on dart2js and from 395 ns to 215 ns on dart2wasm. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.config_test.dart`, group `adapt-resolver-cached-read` | Compare the `get` traps and `_cached` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-resolver-single-walk"></a>`adapt-resolver-single-walk` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.config.dart` `_createSubResolver`, `_addScopesFromKey`, `_addScopes`, `_plainScopeKey`, `_plainScopeRead`, `_plainScope`, `_plainFallback` | `src/helpers/helpers.config.ts`@v4.6.1:309-333, 335-358, 360-371 | adapt | `createSubResolver` walks `[...parentScopes, ...rootScopes]`. For the sub-resolver of a top-level resolver (a plugin, the chart options, a scale), both are one list, so `addScopes` walks each scope two times. The port copies the list once and walks it a second time only when that time could differ. It skips the second time when the first time ran to its end and each of its steps ran no code of the user and reads the same values again: the key is `true` or a name with no dot; each parent is a primitive value, a Map of the SDK (`LinkedHashMap`, the class of a Map literal) or a typed scope with the face of its class (`OptionScope.nativeFace`) and no route of the key; each scope that it finds is such a Map, such a typed scope with no route of `_fallback`, or a primitive; and each `_fallback` is a string, a boolean, `null` or `undefined`. Then the second time finds the same scopes, which the `Set` already holds, and meets the same fallbacks, which did not end the first time. A function `_fallback` (a call of the user), a Map of a user class or a subclass of a scope class (a read of the user), a route (a new merged object at each read, `core.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:144-153) and a dotted key take the second time, as upstream. The scopes, their order, the fallbacks and the calls of the user are the upstream ones. Only Dart calls with no JavaScript counterpart can run fewer times: the key equality of a `LinkedHashMap` made with `equals` and `hashCode` functions, and the `==` of a `Set` element. The change is for speed only (zoom profiles of 2026-11-10: `addScopesFromKey` was 2.2-5.1% of a 1000-point input step). | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.config_test.dart`, group `adapt-resolver-single-walk` | Compare `createSubResolver`, `addScopes` and `addScopesFromKey` on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-resolver-objects"></a>`js-semantics-resolver-objects` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.config.dart` `Resolver` and `ContextResolver` (the `getPrototypeOf` traps), `_cached`, `_set`; `lib/src/js/js_object.dart` `jsGet`; `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `_updateStacks`, `stacksOf`; `test/specs/helpers.config_test.dart` cases `should support common object methods` of `_createResolver` and `_attachContext` | `src/helpers/helpers.config.ts`@v4.6.1:77-82, 165-170, 218-225; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:165, 461-463; `src/controllers/controller.polarArea.js`@v4.6.1:232-236; `test/specs/helpers.config.tests.js`@v4.6.1:97, 752 | js-semantics | Upstream builds the resolvers as `Proxy` objects over JavaScript objects. The port builds them as typed classes with a face (`JsProxyObject`) over typed scopes and Maps ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §4, §5). They have no prototype. The `getPrototypeOf` traps have no port. Thus the two spec expects `instanceof Options` have no Dart form. `_cached` gives `undefined` for the key `constructor`. Upstream gives `Object`. The members of `Object.prototype`, for example `toString`, resolve as absent keys. Thus the `ToPrimitive` of a resolver with a context (`_attachContext`) has no upstream throw. In the reference engine, the `ToNumber` of an option that resolves to a plain object calls `Object.prototype.valueOf` through `_resolveScriptable` with `this` undefined and throws a `TypeError`. An example is the polar area `angle: [90, {}]` at `controller.polarArea.js:234`. The port gives NaN (an arc `endAngle` of NaN). A `_createResolver` without a context gives NaN in both. A scope can also be an array, a function or a primitive (`createSubResolver`, :335-358). In the port, an array or a string scope has only its indices and `length`. Other values have no property. A class value (`JsClass`) is a function scope. A read gives its static properties through `jsGet`, as upstream does. The `has`, `getOwnPropertyDescriptor` and `ownKeys` traps find no static property. Upstream finds the own static properties: `Object.keys` of a resolver over `class A { static id = 'a'; }` gives `['id']`. No option scope of Chart.js is a class value. In the reference engine, the item resolvers of an array option also read the `Array.prototype` methods from the array scope. For example, `fill` resolves to `Array.prototype.fill`, and its scriptable call throws a `TypeError`. A property set works only on an object: a Map or a typed object with a face. A set on an array or a function target throws a `TypeError`. JavaScript adds the property. A Map takes only the values that its value type allows. A nested Map literal can have a narrow inferred type, for example `Map<String, int>` for `{'a': 1}`. A write of another type through a resolver (the `set` traps and `subGetTarget`, :101-106, 189-193, 373-388) then throws a `TypeError`. JavaScript sets the property. A scope that takes writes is a typed scope or has the runtime type `Map<String, Object?>`. Option objects that are plain objects give the same results. The same throw applies to the data items that ported code writes into. With `parsing: false` and a stacked scale, `updateStacks` writes `item._stacks = {}` into each data item (:165). A data item with a narrow inferred type, for example `Map<String, double>` for `{'x': 0.0, 'y': 1.0}`, then throws a `TypeError`. A data item of the runtime type `Map<String, Object?>` takes the write. The stacks stay in the user item, as upstream reads `parsed._stacks` from the same object. A List, a `DateTime` or a function data item takes `_stacks` through `stacksOf` (an `Expando`). A class value takes it as an own static property. A primitive data item throws a `TypeError`, as upstream (Chart.js ADR 0036). With `parsing: false`, `meta.parsed` is the user List. A typed user List, for example `List<double>`, takes no Map: when `parsing` then changes to true, `parse` throws a `TypeError` at the first write, where upstream writes the parsed objects into the user array. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.config_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-resolver-objects`; `test/port/core/core.datasetController_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-resolver-objects`; `test/port/core/core.datasetController_chart_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-resolver-objects`; `test/port/js/js_number_chart_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-resolver-objects` | Compare the traps, `_cached`, `needsSubResolver` and `createSubResolver` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-defaults-route"></a>`adapt-defaults-route` | `lib/src/core/core.defaults.dart` `Defaults.route`, `Defaults._typedScope`; `lib/src/options/option_scope.dart` `OptionScope.addRoute` | `src/core/core.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:130-157 | adapt | Upstream calls `Object.defineProperties` on the scope object. It defines a hidden property `_<name>` for the local value and an enumerable accessor `<name>` that reads the route. A Dart object has no accessor properties. The port gives a typed scope a typed route of the name (`OptionScope.addRoute`, [Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §3): the own cell of `<name>` is the local value, and a read of `<name>` reads the route. A scope object or a target scope object that is a raw Map (an object of user code in the defaults) becomes a typed scope with the same keys, values and key order in the place of the Map in the object that owns it (`_typedScope`); the kind is the kind of its name in a typed owner, else a kind with no declared key. Usually the owner is the parent scope. When the parent scope reads the Map through a route, the owner is the target scope of that route, and the local value of the parent does not change. A route keeps its target scope object, as upstream does. The port keeps the `configurable` attribute of `Object.defineProperties`: a name that the scope did not own before its first route throws a `TypeError` on a second route and on a delete, and the failed second route stores the routed value as the local value. Three differences remain. Code that kept a reference to a replaced Map holds an object that is no longer in the defaults. A program can read `scope._<name>` in JavaScript, and it gets no such key in the port. If the scope already has an own key `_<name>`, upstream writes the local value into that key, and a later write to `_<name>` changes the local value of `<name>`. In the port, the key `_<name>` keeps its value and has no effect on `<name>`. Chart.js reads none of these. | — | `test/port/core/core.defaults_test.dart`, group `adapt-defaults-route` | Compare `route` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-defaults-scope-object"></a>`adapt-defaults-scope-object` | `lib/src/core/core.defaults.dart` `_getScope`, `_set`, `Defaults.get`, `Defaults.route`; `lib/src/core/core.typedRegistry.dart` `TypedRegistry.unregister` | `src/core/core.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:15-32, 97-99, 130-157; `src/core/core.typedRegistry.js`@v4.6.1:75-76 | adapt | Upstream walks a scope path through any value. A falsy value gets a new object. A truthy primitive at the end of the path is the result: `defaults.set('color', {})` merges nothing and returns `'#666'`. Each node in the port is an object: a typed scope or a `Map<String, Object?>` ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §3), and the results of `set`, `describe`, `override` and `get` are such an object (`index.d.ts:713-718` declares `AnyObject`). Thus a truthy value that is not an object throws a `TypeError` at any place in the path. Upstream also throws a `TypeError` when it must create a scope in a primitive, for example for `defaults.set('color.x', {})`. Upstream adds the scope to an array or a function node. A truthy property of a string node, for example `length`, continues the upstream walk. `Defaults.route` also needs an object scope. Upstream routes an array scope or a function scope: after `defaults.route('events', 'x', '', 'color')`, `defaults.events.x` is `'#666'`. The port throws a `TypeError` for such a scope. Both throw for a primitive scope, and both create the target scope before the throw. `TypedRegistry.unregister` also needs an object at `defaults[scope]`. Upstream tests `id in defaults[scope]` on any object, so an array scope gives no throw there. The port throws a `TypeError` for an array or a function scope. Both throw for a primitive and for `undefined`. Paths through objects give the same results. | — | `test/port/core/core.defaults_test.dart`, group `adapt-defaults-scope-object`; `test/port/core/core.typedRegistry_test.dart`, group `adapt-defaults-scope-object` | Compare `getScope`, `set`, `get` and `TypedRegistry.unregister` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-typed-options"></a>`adapt-typed-options` | `lib/src/core/core.defaults.dart` (file marker) `defaults`, `overrides`, `descriptors`, `Defaults`, `Defaults.route`, `_getScope`; `lib/src/core/core.typedRegistry.dart` `_registerDefaults`; `lib/src/helpers/helpers.config.dart` (file marker) `OptionResolver`, `OptionView`, `createResolver`, `attachContext`; `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `resolveElementNamedOptions`, `_mergeScaleConfig`; `lib/src/options/` (port-only: `Opt`, `OptionScope`, the generated scopes and resolved options classes) | `src/core/core.defaults.js`@v4.6.1:7-175; `src/core/core.typedRegistry.js`@v4.6.1:84-101; `src/helpers/helpers.config.ts`@v4.6.1:1-456; `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:71-113, 351-367 | adapt | The typed representation of [Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §1-§3. Upstream keeps the global scopes as plain objects. The port keeps them as typed option scopes: `defaults` is a `ChartOptionsScope`, `overrides` an `OverridesByTypeScope` and `descriptors` a `DescriptorsScope`, and each nested object of the built-in defaults is a typed scope of the kind of its key. A key that the kind does not declare is an extra of its scope. A scope keeps the own-key order of JS. A typed scope that `_getScope` makes for an absent node is of the kind of its key; another new node is a raw Map, the `{}` of upstream. `registerDefaults` merges into a new typed scope of the kind of the scope node in place of `Object.create(null)`. `route` on a typed scope gives the scope a route of the key (`OptionScope.addRoute`) with the getter and the setter of `Object.defineProperties`: the local value, the target value, or a merge of both objects, at each access. The values, the key order, the routes and the throws are the upstream ones. A typed scope is not a `Map`: it reads and writes by name through its face (`JsObjectFace`, Chart.js ADR 0054 §5). Upstream resolves the options through `Proxy` objects over a cache object. The port has the typed classes `OptionResolver` (the target of `_createResolver`) and `OptionView` (the target of `_attachContext`) with the slots of the cache object as fields, and a cache of the resolved values by `OptionKey` (§4). The walk reads a typed scope with `OptionScope.lookup` and another scope with `jsGet`. A write through a resolver into a typed scope classifies the value. A new nested object of `subGetTarget` in a typed scope is a typed scope of the kind of its key. The element step of `_resolveElementOptions` (`resolveElementNamedOptions`) reads each name of `defaults.elements[type]` with the typed read `readKey` and stores it into the resolved options class of the element type: `PointOptions`, `LineOptions`, `BarOptions`, `ArcOptions` or `GenericElementOptions` (§9). `mergeScaleConfig` gives a typed `ScaleOptionsByIdScope` of `ScaleOptionsScope`s; `mergeIf` into them makes typed children of the kinds of their keys. The resolvers and the views have the face of §5 (`jsRead`, `jsWrite`, `jsKeys`, `jsHas`, `jsDelete`) over their traps. The values, the key order, the user calls and the throws are the upstream ones. | — | `test/port/options/schema_test.dart` (the defaults inventory), `test/port/core/core.defaults_test.dart`, `test/port/core/resolver_trace_test.dart` (D2), `test/port/options/observation_test.dart` (O1 and O3) | Compare `core.defaults.js`, `helpers.config.ts` and `resolveNamedOptions` on each bump; edit the schema for the keys and the component file for the values. |

| <a id="adapt-create-context-prototype"></a>`adapt-create-context-prototype` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.options.dart` `ContextObject`, `createContext` | `src/helpers/helpers.options.ts`@v4.6.1:202-206 | adapt | Upstream makes the context with `Object.assign(Object.create(parentContext), context)`. A Dart object has no prototype. Thus the port makes a `ContextObject`, a typed object with a face ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §5, §11) with the own properties and a `prototype` object (a context or a Map). `jsRead` and `operator []` read the own value, else the value of the prototype chain, else `undefined`; they also find an inherited `null`. `hasProperty` is the `in` operator: it is also true for a key of the prototype chain. `jsHas` is `hasOwnProperty`. `jsKeys` and `jsObjectKeys` give the own keys only, as `Object.keys` does. A `for...in` loop of upstream also gives the inherited keys. Upstream does not loop over a context. The port has no `Object.getPrototypeOf`. The `prototype` field gives the parent. A write and a delete change the own properties only, as upstream. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.options_test.dart`, group `adapt-create-context-prototype` | Compare `createContext` and the readers of the context objects on each bump: `helpers.config.ts`, `helpers.segment.js` and the `getContext` methods. |
| <a id="adapt-typed-contexts"></a>`adapt-typed-contexts` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.options.dart` `createContext`, `ScriptableChartContext`, `ScriptableContext`, `ScriptableScaleContext`, `ScriptableScalePointLabelContext`, `ScriptableTooltipContext`, `ScriptableLineSegmentContext`; `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `_createDatasetContext`, `_createDataContext`, `getContext` | `src/helpers/helpers.options.ts`@v4.6.1:202-206; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:182-205, 762-776; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:23-42, 1419-1431, 2789-2793, 3252-3255 | adapt | Upstream makes each context as a plain object with a prototype. The port makes the typed class of the `type` of the context ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §11): `chart` gives a `ScriptableChartContext`, `dataset` and `data` a `ScriptableContext`, `scale` and `tick` a `ScriptableScaleContext`, `pointLabel` a `ScriptableScalePointLabelContext`, `tooltip` a `ScriptableTooltipContext` and `segment` a `ScriptableLineSegmentContext`. Another type gives a `ContextObject`. Each member of the object literal of upstream is a typed field. Another own key is an extra. The own keys keep their insertion order, and `Object.keys` gives the array-index keys first, as JavaScript does. A read of a member that the context does not own reads the prototype chain. The values, the key order and the reads of the parent are the ones of `adapt-create-context-prototype`. The classes are internal: `lib/helpers.dart` exports `ContextObject` only. The dataset controller makes its dataset and data contexts with the typed constructors `ScriptableContext.dataset` and `ScriptableContext.data` (the keys of the literals of `createDatasetContext` and `createDataContext` in their order), and `getContext` writes them with the typed setters. `getContext` reads the context of a data element through the typed `Element.$context`: a value that it does not give (a value that is not a context, or for a built-in element class a context that is not a `ScriptableContext`), which only user code can write into `element.$context`, reads as absent, and a new data context takes its place, where upstream writes into the value (and throws for a primitive). | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.options_test.dart`, group `adapt-typed-contexts` | Compare the object literals of the `create*Context` functions and the context interfaces of `index.d.ts` on each bump. |

Another data item of `parsing: false`, a `_stacks` or `_custom` object that user code gave, and a value of another type that user code wrote into these private objects read and write by name. The direct writes of line and scatter, and the line writes of line, scatter and radar, use the typed setters of the port-only interfaces `types.PointElementWrites` and `types.LineElementWrites` for an element of the exact class `PointElement` or `LineElement` (`Element.typedWrites`), and writes by name for another class; the controllers import no element module (Chart.js ADR 0028 §1). `_cachedDataOpts` is two typed Maps with the upstream keys. The port maps a hook name of the `Plugin` interface to `PluginHook` ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §12) and passes another name by name. A typed `Plugin` gives the value of its hook field; a function of the type of the hook gets a typed call, another function value a call with JS arity, `this` the plugin. A Map plugin is a `MapPlugin`: `map[hook]` at each notify and a call with JS arity, `this` the Map, as upstream. A class value reads by name. `chart.notifyPlugins` passes `args` by identity. Only a hook function of a typed `Plugin` that is typed for the argument class of its hook (a built-in plugin after lane L4) throws a `TypeError` for an args object of another class. The seven built-in plugins are `Plugin` instances whose hook fields hold private functions of the hook type `(chart, args, options)`; the JS `length` of a built-in hook is 3 (the upstream counts of Chart.js ADR 0038 §3 are no behavior). The hooks `afterEvent` of the legend and of the tooltip take `EventHookArgs`, `beforeDatasetDraw` of the filler takes `DatasetDrawHookArgs`, and the other built-in hooks take any args object. Thus a public `notifyPlugins('afterEvent', map)` or `notifyPlugins('beforeDatasetDraw', map)` with a Map `map` throws a `TypeError` in these three hooks, where upstream reads the Map. The legend items of the built-in `generateLabels`, the hit boxes, the column sizes, the tooltip items, body items, label colors, label point styles and positions are typed records with the upstream keys in the upstream order and the face. A user object in their place is read by name. Upstream elements are open objects that controllers write with `Object.assign` of a literal and that animations read and write by name. In the port each numeric property is a `double` field (NaN when undefined) and a bit of an `int` mask; the face gives `undefined` for a set bit. `Element.assign` of a property object writes its own keys in `Object.keys` order through the typed setters, for an element of the built-in class only (`typedWrites`, the exact-class rule); an extra key, a foreign value and a user subclass take the writes by name. Ported `getProps` callers (also `core.interaction` through `numberOf`, the typed current value of a property of an element of any class) read the fields or the `to` of an active animation (`finalNumber`, `finalValue`). Element code reads resolved options through the typed fields with the presence bit and the foreign slot (`plainOption`, `numberOption`, `rawOption`); a plain options Map reads by name. `Element.$context` is a `ContextObject?` with a covariant setter, narrowed to `ScriptableContext?` in the four element classes; the face keeps any value. `Animations` keeps `_properties` by `AnimatedProp` id with the JS Map key order; `_createAnimations` walks a property object by its typed keys from the last one; the number path writes an element through its typed setter. A segment style is a `SegmentStyle`, a typed record with the face. Values, key order, writes, user calls and throws are the upstream ones. Reason: performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1) and the decision of the maintainer (Chart.js ADR 0054). | — | `test/port/core/core.element_test.dart` (`markUndefined`, the id constants, `$context`); `test/port/elements/element.point_test.dart`, group `typed writes`; `test/port/elements/element.bar_test.dart`, the `BorderSkipped` test; `test/port/core/core.animations_test.dart`, group `typed keys`; `test/port/options/observation_test.dart` (O1) | Compare `core.element.ts`, the four element files, `core.animations.js`, `core.animation.js` and `readStyle` on each bump; the property objects of the controllers follow the literals of `updateElements`. |
| <a id="js-semantics-segment-style-json"></a>`js-semantics-segment-style-json` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.segment.dart` `_serializeJsonProperty` | `src/helpers/helpers.segment.js`@v4.6.1:349-364 | js-semantics | `styleChanged` compares the `JSON.stringify` text of two style objects. JavaScript writes an object of a class with its own enumerable properties. Chart.js ADR 0043 makes such an object a leaf value, and a Dart object has no enumerable properties. Thus the port writes `{}` for every object that is not a `Map`, a `List`, a pattern or a gradient. A `SegmentStyle`, the style object of `readStyle` ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §9), writes its own keys in their order from its typed fields. Two different leaf objects in the same style key give no style change. For example, a `borderColor` of an object `{v: 1}` of a class and then an object `{v: 2}` of the same class gives three segments in JavaScript and one segment in the port. A cycle in a style value overflows the stack, where JavaScript throws a `TypeError`. The style options of Chart.js are strings, numbers, arrays of numbers, patterns and gradients. These give the same results. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.segment_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-segment-style-json` | Compare `readStyle` and `styleChanged` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-dom-supported"></a>`platform-dom-supported` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.dom.dart` `isDomSupported`, `supportsEventListenerOptions` | `src/helpers/helpers.dom.ts`@v4.6.1:9-11, 249-267 | platform | Upstream tests for the globals `window` and `document`. | <a id="platform-dom-container"></a>`platform-dom-container` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.dom.dart` `getParentNode`, `_getContainerSize`; `lib/src/canvas/canvas.dart` `CanvasNode`, `CanvasContainer` | `src/helpers/helpers.dom.ts`@v4.6.1:16-22, 136-147 | platform | Upstream takes the DOM `parentNode` of the canvas, or the `host` of a `ShadowRoot` parent. It measures the container with `getBoundingClientRect()` minus padding and border. Without a parent node, it reads `canvas.clientWidth` and `canvas.clientHeight`. The port takes the parent node from the port-only interface `CanvasNode.parentNode`. The canvas element of a chart host implements that interface. The parent node is a `CanvasContainer`, whose `contentWidth` and `contentHeight` are the max sizes of the host's incoming constraints (`double.infinity` when unbounded). A canvas that does not implement `CanvasNode`, or has no container, gives 0 x 0: the DOM gives a canvas outside the document a `clientWidth` and `clientHeight` of 0. | <a id="platform-relative-position"></a>`platform-relative-position` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.dom.dart` `getRelativePosition`, `_getCanvasPosition` | `src/helpers/helpers.dom.ts`@v4.6.1:65-131 | platform | Upstream subtracts the CSS padding of the canvas (and its border for a `clientX` position), corrects for `box-sizing: border-box`, and scales by `/ width * canvas.width / currentDevicePixelRatio`. It takes the position from `offsetX`/`offsetY` when `useOffsetPos` allows, else from `clientX`/`clientY` minus `getBoundingClientRect()`. The port takes the position of a port-only `NativeEvent` (`lib/src/platform/native_event.dart`): the first entry of `touches`, else `offsetX`/`offsetY`. The `touches` of a `NativeEvent` hold only the touch pointers that went down on the chart host, sorted by pointer id (the DOM `targetTouches`). The DOM `touches` holds every touch point on the screen. Thus with a finger down outside the canvas, upstream can take the position of that finger (Chart.js ADR 0037, Open 11). Both are in the logical pixels of the chart, from `PointerEvent.localPosition` (Chart.js ADR 0037). It keeps the `Math.round` (`jsRound`) of each coordinate. It has no CSS offsets, no `clientX` fallback and no scale factor. `retinaScale` gives the canvas `floor(round1(width * dpr))` device pixels, so the factor is `floor(round1(width * dpr)) / (width * dpr)`. For a fractional device width the result can differ from upstream by 1 px (for example, width 300.5 at DPR 1 and offsetX 150.6 give 151, where upstream gives 150). A `touchend` after the last finger lifts gives NaN, as upstream does. For a `ChartEvent`, upstream returns the event itself. The port returns a `Point` that reads the event's `x` and `y`. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.dom_test.dart`, group `platform-relative-position`; spec cases `diverges: platform-relative-position` | Compare `getRelativePosition` and `getCanvasPosition` on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-dom-no-css"></a>`platform-dom-no-css` | `lib/src/helpers/helpers.dom.dart` `getMaximumSize`, `_getContainerSize` | `src/helpers/helpers.dom.ts`@v4.6.1:29-43, 52-64, 143-149, 169-185 | platform | Upstream reads the computed CSS of the canvas: its margins, `max-width`, `max-height` and `box-sizing`, and for `content-box` its paddings and borders. It also reads the borders, paddings, `max-width` and `max-height` of the container. Flutter has no CSS. The Flutter layout above the chart host (`Padding`, `ConstrainedBox`, `SizedBox`) applies these before the host gets its constraints. Thus the port reads no style: the margins, paddings and borders are 0, the max sizes are `INFINITY`, and the `content-box` branch never runs. `parseMaxStyle` and `getPositionedStyle` have no port. The `round1`, `Math.floor` and `width / 2` steps and the aspect-ratio branches are line-by-line ports. A CSS max size or a percentage max size has no effect. | — | `test/port/helpers/helpers.dom_test.dart`, group `platform-dom-no-css`; spec cases `n/a: Flutter has no CSS max-width`, `n/a: Flutter has no CSS max-height`, `n/a: Flutter has no CSS padding` | Compare `getMaximumSize` and `getContainerSize` on each bump. |

A canvas whose parent node is a `RenderChartHost` gives `FlutterPlatform` (the replacement of `DomPlatform`). | <a id="adapt-line-points-elements"></a>`adapt-line-points-elements` | `lib/src/elements/element.line.dart` `LineElement.points`, `operator []=` (`points`, `_points`); `lib/src/types/index.dart` `LineElement.points`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.segment.dart` `pointsFromSegments`, `segments`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.dart` `computeCircularBoundary`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.stack.dart` `stackResultPoint`, `findPoint`; the `element.point.dart` imports of `filler.segment.dart` and `filler.target.dart` | `src/elements/element.line.js`@v4.6.1:308-317; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:2026; `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.segment.js`@v4.6.1:38-43, 65-82; `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.js`@v4.6.1:90-93; `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.stack.js`@v4.6.1:58-109 | adapt | Upstream declares `points: Point[]` and stores any array. The port keeps a `List<types.PointElement>`, because `helpers.segment` reads `skip` and `stop` of each point and `helpers.curve` writes its control points. Thus a write of `points` with a list that is not a `List<types.Element>`, or a write of `_points` with a list that is not a `List<types.PointElement>`, throws an `ArgumentError`, for example plain `Point`s or the `InterpolatedPoint` results of `interpolate`. Upstream accepts such a list. The setter and the write by name copy a `List<types.Element>` of point elements into a `List<types.PointElement>` (Chart.js ADR 0028). Thus `line.points` and `meta.data` of a line controller are two lists with the same elements in the same order, where upstream shares one array. No reader at the pin sees the difference between `buildOrUpdateElements` and the controller `update` of one `chart.update()`; only a user plugin hook `afterLayout` can. An element that is not a `types.PointElement` throws a `TypeError` at the copy. In Chart.js only the filler writes plain points (`filler.helper.js:28-33`, `filler.target.stack.js:29`). Its port makes a `PointElement` for each plain object, with only the properties of that object ([Chart.js ADR 0040](adr/0040-filler-targets-and-plain-points.md)): `x` and `y` for the points of `_pointsFromSegments`, `x`, `y` and `angle` for the records of `getPointPositionForValue`, the one property of an edge object of `_segments`, `x` and `y` for a plain result of `interpolate`, and no property for an Array result of `interpolate`. A property that the object does not have stays in the `undefined` state: its read by name gives `undefined`, and its getter gives NaN. A point element of a line stays the same object. A `PointElement` without options gives the same `skip`, `stop` (`undefined`) and control points (`adapt-spline-control-point-nan`) as the plain object. Thus the segments, the bounds, the fill sources and the draw calls are those of upstream, also when upstream draws no stacked fill for an Array result. `filler.segment.js` and `filler.target.js` do not import the element modules. Their ports import `element.point.dart` for the wraps. | — | `test/port/elements/element.line_test.dart`, group `adapt-line-points-elements`; `test/port/controllers/controller.line_test.dart` and `test/port/controllers/controller.radar_test.dart`, group `interfaces` › `line.points`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.stack_test.dart`, group `adapt-line-points-elements`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.segment_test.dart`, group `adapt-line-points-elements`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/index_test.dart` and `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target_test.dart` (the reference cases) | Compare the writers of `points` and `_points` on each bump: `plugin.filler` and the dataset controllers. |
| <a id="adapt-filler-target-classes"></a>`adapt-filler-target-classes` | `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.drawing.dart` `_pathSegment`, `interpolatedLineTo`, `drawfill`, `_clipVertical`, `_clipHorizontal`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.segment.dart` `segments`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.stack.dart` `_findPoint`; `lib/src/types/index.dart` `SimpleArc` | `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.drawing.js`@v4.6.1:14-16, 69, 85, 93, 99, 115, 123, 153, 201; `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.segment.js`@v4.6.1:15; `src/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target.stack.js`@v4.6.1:47, 88, 94-95; `src/helpers/helpers.canvas.ts`@v4.6.1:328-333 | adapt | Upstream calls `segments`, `points`, `pathSegment`, `interpolate` and `first` of a fill target by name. The stack fill also reads `interpolate`, `segments` and `points` of each line below by name (`filler.target.stack.js:88-95`). The port dispatches on the interfaces `types.LineElement` and `types.SimpleArc` ([Chart.js ADR 0040](adr/0040-filler-targets-and-plain-points.md) §4). No built-in controller has such an element. A radar fill on a chart without a layout throws a `TypeError` in both. Upstream `clipArea` first calls `ctx.save()` and `ctx.beginPath()` and then throws at `area.left`. The port throws before `clipArea`, so it records two canvas calls less, and upstream keeps an unbalanced `save`. | — | `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.drawing_test.dart`, group `adapt-filler-target-classes`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.filler/filler.target_test.dart`, cases `drawfill radar without a layout` and `drawfill a point element as the target` | Compare the target reads of `filler.drawing.js`, `filler.segment.js` and `filler.target.stack.js`, and `clipArea`, on each bump. |

The ordinary path stays pending as the current path, with its commands, until a later command or read needs it. For the exact class, a native canvas also skips the translation of an offset of 0. | <a id="adapt-sector-run-seams"></a>`adapt-sector-run-seams` | `lib/src/canvas/canvas_ui.dart` `tryDrawArcSector`, `_flushSectorRun`, `_endSectorRun` | `src/elements/element.arc.ts`@v4.6.1:289-310 | adapt | Adjacent fused sectors with one result color, one center, one pair of radii and one rotation, drawn in order in the `srcOver` mode, draw as one `drawArc` below a full turn. A different color ends the run, as does any other draw, clip or end of the recording. **Observable difference:** upstream fills each slice, so the anti-aliased seams between slices of one color show the background; the run has no such seams and draws a solid sector. With slices above a pixel only the pixels on the seams differ: at most 74 of 255 at a contrast of 204 on `flutter_tester` (Skia on the CPU). With slices far below a pixel each separate fill covers little of each pixel and the difference covers the whole disc: 199 of the contrast of 204 at 20000 slices, where the separate fills leave a pale disc and the run a solid one. A renderer with sample coverage has no such seams in the separate fills; it is not measured here. Not on the slices of different colors, which keep exact pixels. The form of Chart.js ADR 0055 §8.5 for one radius gives 178 of 255, 99th percentile 75, on the same radii. Its figure of 46 of 255 holds for its radius of 3 in its color, and this form does not reach that figure. Below a radius of 4 the largest difference of this form is up to 10 above that of the form for one radius. A chart color on white gives 63 of 255. | <a id="adapt-bar-clip"></a>`adapt-bar-clip` | `lib/src/elements/element.bar.dart` `BarElement.draw` | `src/elements/element.bar.js`@v4.6.1:179-200 | adapt | Upstream clips to the outer rectangle before it fills the border ring with the `evenodd` rule (:189). When `inflateAmount` is 0 (also -0, as JS `-0 === 0`), the port does not call `ctx.clip()`. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1): the clip took 14 % of the draw of a bar ([performance.md](performance.md) §8.2). The figure is in [performance.md](performance.md) §8.4. With the amount 0, `inflateRect` gives the rectangles of `boundingRects` with the same numbers. The inner rectangle is inside the outer one: its edges are inside by the border widths, and each border width is from 0 to half the side. Each inner corner arc has a radius that is smaller by the larger of its two border widths, so the arc stays inside the outer arc. A side with a negative length has no border and no radius. Thus the border ring and the fill are inside the clip, and the clip changes no area. A non-finite rectangle adds no path, so neither form draws. The only observable difference is the anti-aliasing of the edges ([Chart.js ADR 0034](adr/0034-no-display-only-js-exactness.md)): with the clip, the coverage of an edge pixel is the product of the coverage of the fill and of the clip. An edge pixel differs by up to 1/4 of a channel. Another amount keeps the clip. `controller.bar.js:252-256` gives 0 for `'auto'` unless the bar fills its category (then 0.33). The mock context records no `clip` call for a bar with the amount 0, and the tests remove `clip` from the reference calls of such a bar. | — | `test/port/elements/element.bar_test.dart`, group `adapt-bar-clip` and group `draw`; `test/port/elements/element.bar_ui_test.dart`; `test/port/scales/scale_chart_harness.dart` `expectCalls` (it removes the reference `clip` of the border ring of each bar that the port draws without a `clip`) and `test/port/scales/scale_chart_harness_test.dart`, group `expectCalls: adapt-bar-clip` | On each bump, compare `draw`, `boundingRects` and `inflateRect` of `element.bar.js`. The row holds only while the inner rectangle stays inside the outer one at the amount 0. |

Under `flutter test` a pure spec runs both restores; they put back the same snapshot, and the second finds nothing to change. | <a id="harness-method-fields"></a>`harness-method-fields` | `lib/src/core/core.controller.dart` `Chart.resize`, `Chart.eventHandler`; `lib/src/core/core.animator.dart` `Animator.refresh`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.dart` `Tooltip.update` | `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:269-275, 1163-1186; `src/core/core.animator.js`@v4.6.1:38-52, 171; `src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.js`@v4.6.1:638-689, 1128, 1167 | harness | Upstream `resize` and `_eventHandler` are methods of the prototype. chartjs-test-utils replaces them as own properties of one chart: `waitForResize` and `afterEvent` (`src/utils.js`@v0.4.0:130-149). `core.controller.tests.js:719` also replaces `chart.resize`. A Dart method cannot change for one object. Thus the port declares both as fields that hold a function. The initializer of each field gives the tear-off of a private method with the upstream body. Each upstream call of `this.resize` (:241, :1017, :1026) and of `this._eventHandler` (:531, :988) calls the field. Thus a replacement sees each call, as a JS own property does. `_eventHandler` drops its underscore, because the harness reads it ([Chart.js ADR 0012](adr/0012-dart-file-naming.md)). `types.Chart` does not declare the two members, because a field cannot implement a method ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 5). Thus `refresh` is a field too: its initializer gives the tear-off of the private method `_refreshMethod` with the upstream body, and `start`, the frame callback and `unmute` call the field. `plugin.tooltip.tests.js:964` and `:1027` replace `tooltip.update` on one tooltip. Thus `Tooltip.update` is a field too ([Chart.js ADR 0039](adr/0039-tooltip-model-and-animation.md) §6), with the optional parameters `[Object? changed, Object? replay]`: upstream reads `changed` only with `&&` and accepts `update()`. Its initializer gives the tear-off of the private method `_updateMethod` with the upstream body. `setActiveElements` (:1128) calls `update(true)` and `handleEvent` (:1167) calls `update(true, replay)` through the field. `types.TooltipModel` does not declare `update`, as `index.d.ts` does not. | — | `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `harness-method-fields`; `test/port/core/core.animator_test.dart`, group `harness-method-fields`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.tooltip_test.dart`, group `harness-method-fields` | Compare the calls of `resize` and `_eventHandler` in `core.controller.js`, the calls of `_refresh` in `core.animator.js`, and the calls of `update` in `plugin.tooltip.js`, on each bump. |
| <a id="harness-chart-matchers"></a>`harness-chart-matchers` | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/src/matchers.dart` `toBeValidChart`, `toBeChartOfSize` | chartjs-test-utils `src/matchers.js`@v0.4.0:106-174 | harness | The row covers three hunks ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 9). First, `actual instanceof Chart` is a test for `types.Chart`, and the two `Object.prototype.toString` tags are tests for `HTMLCanvasElement` and `CanvasRenderingContext2D`, as the row `platform-color-brand` does for the color brands. Second, `window.devicePixelRatio` (:142) is `actual.platform.getDevicePixelRatio()`. For a chart on the Flutter platform, it is the device pixel ratio of the view of its host. Third, `getComputedStyle(canvas)` (:141) is the display size rule of the harness. A side with an inline px style has that value. With one px style only, the other side keeps the aspect ratio of the render size, as CSS does for a replaced element. Without a px style, a side has the render size. A canvas with its own `display: none` gives `auto` for a side without a px style. `parseInt(..., 10) \|\| 0` truncates toward zero, and `auto` gives 0. The messages and their order are the upstream messages. | — | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/matchers_test.dart`, group `harness-chart-matchers`, with the Chrome values of Chart.js ADR 0022, section 9 | Compare `src/matchers.js` when the chartjs-test-utils pin changes. |
| <a id="harness-acquire-chart"></a>`harness-acquire-chart` | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/src/utils.dart` `acquireChart`, `releaseChart` | chartjs-test-utils `src/utils.js`@v0.4.0:36-115 | harness | Upstream `_acquireChart` appends a `div` wrapper with a canvas to `document.body`. Then it creates the chart in the same task. The browser lays out the canvas when `getMaximumSize` reads its container. The port has five hunks ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 4). First, the function throws a `StateError` outside the body of a `testWidgets` test, before the first DOM write. Second, the forced build and layout of the harness body (`forceHarnessLayout` of `dom.dart`) runs after `document.body.appendChild(wrapper)`. Thus the chart finds its canvas in a laid-out chart host, and the chart is attached at its construction, as upstream. During build, layout or paint, the forced layout throws a `StateError`, and the wrapper leaves the body again. Third, `useOffscreenCanvas: true` gives the chart the context of a second `UiCanvasElement` with the size of the canvas. No chart host shows it. Thus `detectPlatform` gives `BasicPlatform`, as for an `OffscreenCanvas`. Fourth, `useShadowDOM: true` throws an `UnsupportedError` in place of `pending()`. Its spec cases skip as `'n/a: Flutter has no shadow DOM'`. Fifth, `spriteText` and `spritingOff` have no port, because the port compares no image ([Chart.js ADR 0034](adr/0034-no-display-only-js-exactness.md)). The other lines are line-by-line ports. The `Expando` `$test` stands for the own property `chart.$test`. | — | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/utils_test.dart`, group `harness-acquire-chart`; `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/index_test.dart`, group `the registry charts` | Compare `src/utils.js` when the chartjs-test-utils pin changes. |
| <a id="harness-trigger-mouse-event"></a>`harness-trigger-mouse-event` | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/src/utils.dart` `triggerMouseEvent` | chartjs-test-utils `src/utils.js`@v0.4.0:163-184 | harness | Upstream makes a `MouseEvent` at `clientX: rect.left + point.x` and `clientY: rect.top + point.y`, with `cancelable: true` and `bubbles: true`. It dispatches the event on the canvas and waits for the promise of `afterEvent`. The port makes the same event and calls `node.dispatchEvent(event)`, the extension member of `CanvasEventTarget` in `chart_host.dart` ([Chart.js ADR 0108](adr/0108-input-surface-and-exports-for-add-on-plugins.md)). `rect` is `node.getBoundingClientRect()`: the box of the last layout of the chart host, in the harness body, which has no margin. The read does not force a layout. The dispatch calls the listeners of the canvas and then, in the bubble phase, the listeners of the document of `canvas.ownerDocument`, without a hit test and without the gesture arena, as the DOM call does. No Chart.js listener is on the document. The port has two hunks ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 6, and Chart.js ADR 0108). First, the event is a `NativeEvent` that also has `offsetX` and `offsetY` at the point. For a canvas without a border, the DOM `offsetX` is the same point. Chrome rounds the offsets of a synthetic `MouseEvent` to integers. `getRelativePosition` applies `Math.round` to the offsets ([differences.md](#platform-relative-position)). Thus the chart event gets the Chrome position, and only `event.native.offsetX` keeps the fraction. `view: window` has no port: no listener reads it. Second, `await promise` pumps frames of 16 ms until the `afterEvent` callback runs (`pumpFrames` of `dom.dart`). One frame is enough. A type without a listener fails with the Jasmine timeout message after 5000 ms, as upstream. `test/port/platform/chart_host_test.dart` covers the Flutter input path. | — | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/utils_test.dart`, group `harness-trigger-mouse-event`, with the Chrome positions of Chart.js ADR 0022, probe SP1, the bubble phase and the client point of a wrapper at `left: 50px; top: 30px` | Compare `src/utils.js` when the chartjs-test-utils pin changes. |
| <a id="harness-release-at-unmount"></a>`harness-release-at-unmount` | `test/flutter_test_config.dart` `testExecutable` (calls `afterEachChart` and `tearDown` with `releaseCharts` of `package:chartjs_test_utils/flutter.dart`) | `test/index.js`@v4.6.1:34-36 | harness | Upstream calls `releaseCharts()` in a global `afterEach`. Jasmine runs it after the `afterEach` blocks of the `describe` blocks. A `tearDown` of flutter_test runs after the end-of-test checks of `testWidgets`. A chart with a running animation or a pending `resizeDelay` timer then fails these checks. Thus the unmount of the harness body calls `releaseCharts` (`afterEachChart` of `dom.dart`). flutter_test disposes the body in the warm-up frame of its `runApp` after the test body. Thus the release runs before the end-of-test checks and before the `tearDown` functions ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 8). A spec `afterEach` that stays a `tearDown` thus runs after the release, the reverse of the Jasmine order. That is safe while such a block reads no chart. A `tearDown(releaseCharts)` is the fallback, because flutter_test keeps the tree after a failed test. It runs before the restore of `harness-defaults-restore`. `releaseCharts` keeps the upstream registry and never deletes a chart. Thus each test destroys the charts of the earlier tests of the file again, as upstream does after each spec. | — | `test/port/flutter_test_config_test.dart`, group `harness-release-at-unmount`; `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/index_test.dart`, group `harness-release-at-unmount` | Compare `test/index.js` on each bump. |
| <a id="harness-register-builtins"></a>`harness-register-builtins` | `test/flutter_test_config.dart` `testExecutable` (calls `setUpBuiltins`); `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/pure.dart` `setUpBuiltins`, `removeBuiltins`, `setUpSpecs` | `src/index.umd.ts`@v4.6.1:28, `karma.conf.cjs`@v4.6.1:110, `test/index.js`@v4.6.1:25-28 | harness | The upstream specs run on the UMD entry `src/index.umd.ts` (`karma.conf.cjs:110`). It registers the built-in components before the first spec: `Chart.register(controllers, scales, elements, plugins)`. `test/index.js` has no such line. The port makes the same call before `main()` of each test file and before the snapshot of `harness-defaults-restore` ([Chart.js ADR 0022](adr/0022-chart-test-harness.md), section 8). The call is `Chart.register([controllers, scales, elements, plugins])` with the namespace Maps of `lib/src/index.dart`. In phase 8, `plugins` holds the seven plugins in the order of `src/plugins/index.js:1-7`, which is the hook order (`core.plugins.js:125-131`). After the call, the file ports `test/index.js:25-28`: `defaults.plugins.colors.enabled = false`, before the snapshot of `harness-defaults-restore`. The reference generators register the same components with `registerBuiltins` of `tool/reference/scale_chart_common.mjs`, and the two source generators register the seven plugins by name ([Chart.js ADR 0041](adr/0041-plugin-test-strategy.md), section 1). The restore does not reset the `items` of the registry, as upstream. `dart test` does not run `flutter_test_config.dart`. Thus `setUpSpecs()` of a pure-layer file calls `removeBuiltins()`. It unregisters the built-in components and puts back the scopes of before the registration. Thus a pure-layer file has the same state on the VM, dart2js and dart2wasm. Its own registration through a new `TypedRegistry` also does not throw. A spec file with chart cases does not call `setUpSpecs()`. | — | `test/port/flutter_test_config_test.dart`, group `harness-register-builtins` (the plugin ids and the colors line); `packages/chartjs_test_utils/test/pure_test.dart` (the removal of the colors plugin) | Compare `src/index.umd.ts` and `karma.conf.cjs` on each bump. Each phase that ports a built-in component adds it to its namespace Map. |
| <a id="harness-fixture-sources"></a>`harness-fixture-sources` | `packages/chartjs_test_utils/lib/src/fixture.dart` `specsFromFixtures`, `FixtureSource`; `test/fixtures/index.dart` `handFixtures`, `fixtureSkips`; `test/fixtures/hand/scale.time/*.dart` (the `moment` values); `test/fixtures/hand/controller.polarArea/pointLabels/overlapping.dart` (`run`); `test/fixtures/fixture_events.dart` `fixtureMouseMove` and the hand ports `test/fixtures/hand/plugin.tooltip/*.dart` (the raw `mousemove`) | chartjs-test-utils `src/fixture.js`@v0.4.0:3-34, 73-93; the inline `afterDraw` of `test/fixtures/plugin.tooltip/*.js`@v4.6.1 (for example `box-padding.js:44-61`) | harness | Upstream loads each config from the Karma files: `JSON.parse` for a `.json` file and `new Function` for a `.js` module, at each `it`. Dart runs no JavaScript. The runner reads the list from the manifest of tool 4, a pure data config from `doc/upstream/fixtures.json`, and an impure config from a hand port of `test/fixtures/hand/` ([Chart.js ADR 0029](adr/0029-fixture-smoke-runner.md) §3-§5). A data config that holds a keyword of an option with a class value also has a hand port ([Chart.js ADR 0115](adr/0115-hand-ports-of-typed-data-fixtures.md)). Before a config loads, the runner compares the SHA-256 of `third_party/Chart.js/test/fixtures/<path>` with the `sha256` of the manifest item. The check also accepts the bytes with each CRLF changed to LF, because a checkout with `core.autocrlf=true` writes CRLF line ends into the submodule. Without the checkout, each fixture skips with `'n/a: the submodule third_party/Chart.js is not checked out'`; with `FIXTURES_REQUIRED=1`, it fails. The reason names the `submodule` of the `FixtureSource`: the source of an add-on port names its own submodule ([Chart.js ADR 0108](adr/0108-input-surface-and-exports-for-add-on-plugins.md)). The test name is the path relative to `test/fixtures/`, not the Karma URL. The description default and the failure messages use the same relative path, not the Karma path `/base/test/fixtures/<path>`. Port-only checks: a hand port with another `sha256` than the manifest fails as stale; a `blocked: plugin.<id>` skip of a registered plugin fails as stale; an impure fixture without a hand port fails; a hand or skip key that is not a fixture throws a `StateError`. A `moment(...)` value of a hand port is a `DateTime` of the same time value in UTC (`test/index.js`@v4.6.1:23). The hand port of `controller.polarArea/pointLabels/overlapping.js` keeps the line `chart.ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled = false` (:25) as a comment: the canvas layer has no `imageSmoothingEnabled`, and the port draws no text sprite and compares no image, so the write changes no result. The 8 fixtures of `plugin.tooltip` make the raw event `{type: 'mousemove', target: canvas, clientX, clientY}` at a data point and give it to `chart._handleEvent`, `chart.tooltip.handleEvent` and `chart.tooltip.draw`. The hand ports give the chart event that `getRelativePosition` gives for that event (`fixtureMouseMove` of `test/fixtures/fixture_events.dart`): at DPR 1, with the canvas at the origin of its host, `Math.round` of the point (`helpers.dom.ts:128-129`), and `native` Dart `null`, because the raw event has no `native` key ([Chart.js ADR 0041](adr/0041-plugin-test-strategy.md) §10 item 2). One value differs: upstream `tooltip._eventPosition` is `{x: undefined, y: undefined}` for the raw event, and the port has the numbers. Only the positioner `nearest` reads it, and the 8 fixtures use `average`. | <a id="js-semantics-object-freeze"></a>`js-semantics-object-freeze` | `lib/src/js/js_object.dart` `JsFreezable`, `jsObjectFreeze`, `jsObjectIsFrozen`; `lib/src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.dart` `Tooltip._resolveAnimations` | `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:831, 858; `src/core/core.layouts.js`@v4.6.1:395; `src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.js`@v4.6.1:519; `test/specs/core.datasetController.tests.js`@v4.6.1:975, 997, 1018 | js-semantics | JavaScript freezes each object. In strict mode, a write into a frozen object then throws a `TypeError`. A Dart object has a frozen state only when it implements `JsFreezable` ([Chart.js ADR 0021](adr/0021-typed-options-in-the-resolver.md) §5). `jsObjectFreeze` leaves another object writable. Examples are a plain Map, a `List` and an `Animations`. `jsObjectIsFrozen` gives false for such an object, also for a `Map.unmodifiable`. It gives true for Dart `null`, `undefined`, a number, a string and a boolean, as for a JavaScript primitive. The options objects of `lib/src/elements/element_options.dart` implement `JsFreezable`. Thus the cache entries of `_resolveElementOptions` (`core.datasetController.js:831`) keep the upstream throw. No upstream code writes into the objects of the other three `Object.freeze` sites after the freeze. For a chart, the options of the tooltip are a context resolver with `_cacheable` false. Thus the freeze of `plugin.tooltip.js:519` runs only for options that a user gives to `initialize` without a context ([Chart.js ADR 0039](adr/0039-tooltip-model-and-animation.md) §9). | — | `test/port/js/js_object_test.dart`, groups `jsObjectFreeze` and `jsObjectIsFrozen`; `test/port/elements/element_options_test.dart`, groups `frozen state of …`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.tooltip_test.dart`, group `animations` | Compare each `Object.freeze` and `Object.isFrozen` in `src/` and `test/specs/`. |
| <a id="adapt-typed-element-options"></a>`adapt-typed-element-options` | `lib/src/elements/element_options.dart` `createElementOptions`, `copyOptions`. `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `Config.resolveNamedOptions` (`into`). `lib/src/core/core.animations.dart` `_resolveTargetOptions`. `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `DatasetController._resolveElementOptions`, `_cloneIfNotShared`, `DatasetController.getSharedOptions`. `lib/src/controllers/controller.bubble.dart` `BubbleController.resolveDataElementOptions` | `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:351-352; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:230, 821, 871; `src/core/core.animations.js`@v4.6.1:159; `src/controllers/controller.bubble.js`@v4.6.1:157 | adapt | Upstream makes each options object as a plain object. It copies an options object with `Object.assign`. The port makes the typed class of the element type id: `PointOptions`, `LineOptions`, `BarOptions` or `ArcOptions` ([Chart.js ADR 0021](adr/0021-typed-options-in-the-resolver.md)). Another id gets `GenericElementOptions`. A copy keeps the class of its source: `copyOptions`, and in the dataset controller (`_cloneIfNotShared`, `getSharedOptions`) and the bubble `resolveDataElementOptions` the typed copy `ElementOptions.clone()`. The reason is performance ([porting-flow.md](porting-flow.md) §1.1). The typed reads cut the draw time of 20000 points by 20 % to 30 % ([performance.md](performance.md) §8.4). Chart.js ADR 0021 §8 has the figures of the producers and the copies. The measurement of the chart path in phase 5 adds its figures to [performance.md](performance.md). Each instance gives the Map results of a plain object with the same entries. For the objects of the built-in charts, the key order is the JavaScript order. Two cases differ. A default that a program writes before the registration comes first in the names. The typed class puts it at the place of its known key. In a typed copy with extra keys, `$animations` comes before the extra keys. Only the creation order of the option animations reads this order. | — | `test/port/elements/element_options_test.dart`, groups `createElementOptions`, `clone of …`, `copyOptions` and `adapt-typed-element-options`. `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, `test/port/core/core.animations_test.dart` and `test/port/core/core.datasetController_test.dart`, group `adapt-typed-element-options`. `test/port/controllers/controller.bubble_test.dart`: the reference cases with the active and default options of each point | Compare `resolveNamedOptions`, `_resolveElementOptions`, the element ids, the names of `defaults.elements` and each `Object.assign({}, …)` of an options object. |
| <a id="adapt-component-class-role"></a>`adapt-component-class-role` | `lib/src/core/core.element.dart` `elementCfgArg`, the `cfg` of the `construct` closure of each element class value. `lib/src/core/core.datasetController.dart` `controllerArgs`, the `chart` of the `construct` closure of each controller class value, and the casts of `DatasetController.addElements` and `DatasetController._insertElements`. `lib/src/core/core.controller.dart` the casts of `Chart.buildOrUpdateControllers` and `Chart.buildOrUpdateScales`. `lib/src/plugins/plugin.title.dart` `_titleConfig`, the `construct` of `Title.jsClass`. `lib/src/plugins/plugin.legend.dart` `_legendConfig`, the `construct` of `Legend.jsClass`. `lib/src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.dart` `_tooltipConfig`, the `construct` of `Tooltip.jsClass` | `src/elements/element.point.ts`@v4.6.1:57-59; `src/elements/element.line.js`@v4.6.1:294-296; `src/elements/element.bar.js`@v4.6.1:174-176; `src/elements/element.arc.ts`@v4.6.1:424-426; `src/core/core.controller.js`@v4.6.1:357-367, 442-450, 495-506; `src/core/core.datasetController.js`@v4.6.1:255-260, 399-401, 1008-1010; `src/plugins/plugin.title.js`@v4.6.1:11-27; `src/plugins/plugin.legend.js`@v4.6.1:46-80; `src/plugins/plugin.tooltip.js`@v4.6.1:464-496 | adapt | The `construct` closure of a class value converts its arguments to the parameter types of the Dart constructor ([Chart.js ADR 0020](adr/0020-registry-and-component-classes.md) §3). An element constructor takes a Map `cfg`. An absent or falsy `cfg` gives Dart `null`, as `if (cfg)` skips it. A truthy `cfg` that is not a Map throws a `TypeError`. Upstream copies the own enumerable properties of such a value. For example, `new PointElement('ab')` gets the property `0` with the value `'a'`, and `new PointElement(5)` gets no property. Chart.js passes no such value. A controller constructor takes a `types.Chart`. Another value throws a `TypeError`. Upstream reads `chart.ctx` and calls `chart.getDatasetMeta` (`core.datasetController.js:257`, `:324`). An object with these members works there. The port also casts the result of `jsConstruct` to the class of its role: `DatasetController`, `Scale` or `Element`. Thus each later call stays typed. A class value of another role throws a `TypeError` at the cast. An example is a plain class that `addControllers` registers. Upstream constructs the object and keeps it. It throws a `TypeError` later, at the first absent method. Before that throw, `buildOrUpdateControllers` sets `meta.controller` and ends its loop. `update()` notifies `beforeElementsUpdate` (`core.controller.js:497`). `buildOrUpdateScales` writes `scales[scale.id]` (:364). The port does none of these. Both throw a `TypeError` in the same `update()` call. A value that is not a class value throws at `new` in both. The constructors of `Title`, `Legend` and `Tooltip` take the `config` record ([Chart.js ADR 0038](adr/0038-plugin-modules-class-values-and-interfaces.md) §4). The `construct` closure reads `chart`, `options` and `ctx` of the argument with `jsGet`. An absent argument, `undefined` or `null` throws a `TypeError`, as upstream `config.chart` throws. An `options` value that is not a Map, `undefined` or `null`, or a `ctx` that is not a context, `undefined` or `null`, throws a `TypeError`. Upstream stores such a value. `Title` keeps any `chart` value, because it never reads it. For `Legend` and `Tooltip`, a `chart` that is not a `types.Chart` throws a `TypeError`, also an absent `chart`. `Tooltip` reads no `ctx` of the argument, as upstream (:475-476). Upstream stores the value and reads its members later. | — | `test/port/core/core.element_test.dart` and `test/port/core/core.datasetController_test.dart`, group `adapt-component-class-role`. `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `adapt-component-class-role`. `test/port/plugins/plugin.title_test.dart`, `test/port/plugins/plugin.legend_test.dart` and `test/port/plugins/plugin.tooltip_test.dart`, group `adapt-component-class-role` | Compare the element constructors, the `DatasetController` constructor, `buildOrUpdateControllers`, `buildOrUpdateScales`, `addElements` and `_insertElements`, and the constructors of `Title`, `Legend` and `Tooltip`. |

| <a id="adapt-config-objects"></a>`adapt-config-objects` | `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `_initConfig`, `_initData`, `_initOptions`, `Config.data`, `Config.options`, `Config.pluginScopeKeys`, `Config.resolveNamedOptions`; `lib/src/core/core.plugins.dart` `PluginService._createDescriptors` | `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:115-136, 185-187, 267-275, 351-361; `src/core/core.plugins.js`@v4.6.1:101-107 | adapt | Upstream writes the properties `data`, `options`, `plugins`, `scales`, `datasets` and `labels` into any object. The port needs an object: a `Map<String, Object?>` of the user, or a typed scope of the root, data and options of a typed config ([Chart.js ADR 0054](adr/0054-typed-internal-model.md) §5). A configuration or an `options` value that is an array or a function throws a `TypeError`. Upstream writes the properties into it. A `data` array gives a new Map with `datasets` and `labels`. Upstream writes the two properties into the array, and no Chart.js code reads its items. Thus only the identity of `chart.data` differs. A `data` function throws a `TypeError`. Both throw a `TypeError` for a primitive. The `data` and `options` getters throw a `TypeError` for a value that is not an object. After a set of `null`, the `options` getter throws a `TypeError` until `update()`. Upstream returns `null`. `PluginService._createDescriptors` reads the getter for new descriptors (`core.plugins.js:103`). After a set of `undefined` or `false`, the next new descriptors throw a `TypeError` there. Upstream uses `{}` for `undefined` and makes no descriptors for `false`. Both throw for `null`. The `data` getter throws only after a write into the raw configuration. A context of `resolveNamedOptions` that is not an object throws a `TypeError` when an option needs the context. Upstream gives the value to the scriptable options. The `additionalOptionScopes` of a plugin must be a `List` of strings. Another value throws a `TypeError` in `pluginScopeKeys`. Upstream spreads any iterable value. A key that is not a string then throws a `TypeError` in `getOptionScopes`. Chart.js gives objects, a context function that returns an object, and arrays of strings. | — | `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, group `adapt-config-objects`; `test/port/core/core.plugins_test.dart`, group `adapt-config-objects`; `test/port/core/core.controller_test.dart`, group `adapt-config-objects`. The spec cases `core.controller.tests.js:536`, `:566` and `:581` have `data: []`. The case `:581` passes. The cases `:536` and `:566` skip as `blocked` on a scale of phase 6 and need no `diverges` skip | Compare `initConfig`, `initData`, `initOptions`, the `options` getter, `pluginScopeKeys` and `resolveNamedOptions`, and `_createDescriptors` of `core.plugins.js`, on each bump. |
| <a id="theme-H0"></a>`theme-H0` | `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `themeConfigKey`, `_themeOf`, `chartThemeOf` | `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:151-158 | theme | Upstream has no theme. The chart widget `ChartView` puts the compiled theme map under this key of its own shallow copy of the app config, and in debug mode an assert fails for an app value under it ([Chart.js ADR 0050](adr/0050-public-theme-api.md), "Runtime switch", step 2). `initConfig` returns the same object. Thus the key stays in the configuration. A value that is not a Map is no theme. Without a theme, the hunks H1 to H3 add no scope and no key. It also adds the public reader `chartThemeOf(ChartConfiguration)` for hunk H5 of `plugin.colors` ([Chart.js ADR 0050](adr/0050-public-theme-api.md)). It gives the theme map of a `Config`, and Dart `null` for no theme, a value that is not a Map and a configuration that is not a `Config`. | — | `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, group `theme-H0` ([theming.md](theming.md) §8 test 1, and the two `chartThemeOf` cases). `test/port/theme/precedence_test.dart`, groups `test 1: no theme equals upstream` (test 1 on charts with registered components) and `palette` | Compare `initConfig` and the readers of `_config`. | <a id="theme-H1"></a>`theme-H1` | `lib/src/core/core.config.dart` `_mergeScaleConfig` | `src/core/core.config.js`@v4.6.1:106-110 | theme | Upstream fills each merged scale from `defaults.scales[type]` and `defaults.scale`. Before that line, the port fills the scale from `theme.scales[type]` and `theme.scale` with a separate `mergeIf` call ([theming.md](theming.md) §3.1). `mergeIf` fills only absent keys, in the order of its sources. Thus the two calls give the result of one longer source list. The upstream line stays unchanged. A theme `scales` value that is not a Map gives no source. A theme scale key creates no axis. Without a theme, the scales are the upstream scales. | — | `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, group `theme-H1` ([theming.md](theming.md) §8 test 2, the scale rows). `test/port/theme/precedence_test.dart`, groups `test 1: no theme equals upstream` and `test 2: precedence` (tests 1 and 2 on charts with registered components), `a compiled theme on a radial chart`, `the singleton read at scale.radialLinear.js:14` and `test 5: a theme scale type creates no axis`. Run the group `theme-H1` and the H1 groups of `precedence_test.dart`. |

Without a theme, the hunk adds no scope. The cache of the scope lists keeps the theme scopes until `clearCache()`. `update()` clears the cache. | — | `test/port/core/core.config_test.dart`, group `theme-H2` ([theming.md](theming.md) §8 test 1, and test 2, the element and plugin rows). `test/port/theme/precedence_test.dart`, groups `test 1: no theme equals upstream` and `test 2: precedence` (tests 1 and 2 on charts with registered components) | Compare `getOptionScopes`, `addIfFound` and the four scope key functions.
