| <a id="adapt-date-adapter-prototype"></a>`adapt-date-adapter-prototype` | `lib/src/core/core.adapters.dart` `DateAdapterBase.prototype`, `DateAdapterBase.override` and the eight methods of `DateAdapterBase` | `src/core/core.adapters.ts`@v4.6.1:87-91, 100-128 | adapt | Upstream assigns the members of `override` to `DateAdapterBase.prototype` with `Object.assign`. A Dart class has no prototype object. The port keeps the assigned members in the static Map `DateAdapterBase.prototype`. Each method of `DateAdapterBase` calls its member through `jsCall`, with the adapter as `this`. Without a member, the method runs the body of the class method. The Map starts with the default members of the row [`adapt-date-default-adapter`](#adapt-date-default-adapter). A member that is a Dart function of the exact type of its method gets a direct call without `this`. Any other value goes through `jsCall`. A Dart closure cannot read `this`, so both calls give one result ([Chart.js ADR 0024](adr/0024-dates.md) §1). Thus an override reaches each adapter, also an adapter that exists before the override. A Dart subclass method does not read the Map, as a JavaScript subclass method hides the prototype member. Two differences remain. A member that is not a function throws an `ArgumentError` at the call. Upstream throws a `TypeError`. A member that is not a method of `DateAdapter`, for example `_id`, is only an entry of the Map. Upstream reads it as a property of each adapter. No upstream code reads such a property. | — | `test/port/core/core.adapters_test.dart`, group `adapt-date-adapter-prototype` and its group `typed direct call` | Compare `override` and the methods of `DateAdapterBase` on each bump. Run the group `typed direct call` when a member type of `DateAdapter` changes. |
| <a id="adapt-date-default-adapter"></a>`adapt-date-default-adapter` | `lib/src/core/core.adapters.dart` `DateAdapterBase.prototype` (the initializer); `lib/src/core/date_adapter.dart` `dateAdapterMembers` | `src/core/core.adapters.ts`@v4.6.1:65-67, 90, 102-128; `karma.conf.cjs`@v4.6.1:106-111 | adapt | Chart.js has no date adapter. Without an adapter package, each method of `DateAdapterBase` except `init` throws the abstract error. The port starts `DateAdapterBase.prototype` with the default members of `date_adapter.dart` ([Chart.js ADR 0024](adr/0024-dates.md)). They give the results of chartjs-adapter-moment 1.0.1 on moment 2.29.4, the adapter of the specs (`karma.conf.cjs:106-111`), with the zone of `setDefaultDateZone`. `_date` stays `DateAdapterBase`. An `override` replaces single members, as an `override` after an adapter package does upstream. The members have no `init` and no `_id`. | — | `test/port/core/date_adapter_test.dart`, groups `adapt-date-default-adapter`, `reference UTC` and `reference local` | Compare `core.adapters.ts` and the versions of moment, moment-timezone and chartjs-adapter-moment in `package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml` on each bump. When one moves, run `tool/reference/date_adapter_reference.mjs` with the new versions and `--full`. |
| <a id="adapt-date-moment-format"></a>`adapt-date-moment-format` | `lib/src/core/date_format.dart` `dateFormats`, `formatDate`; `lib/src/core/date_parse.dart` `parseWithFormat` | `src/scales/scale.time.js`@v4.6.1:61-63, 277, 495-544; `karma.conf.cjs`@v4.6.1:111 | adapt | The specs format and parse with moment tokens of chartjs-adapter-moment 1.0.1. The port has no moment-token formatter or parser ([Chart.js ADR 0034](adr/0034-no-display-only-js-exactness.md) §5). `formats()` gives ICU patterns of `DateFormat` with the en_US text of the moment `FORMATS`: the four keys with a day period write it in lower case ([Chart.js ADR 0024](adr/0024-dates.md) §6). A format string is an ICU pattern. A negative year has no sign in an ICU pattern, where moment `YYYY` writes `-0005`. The parse with a format is the `tryParse` of intl: it does not check the field ranges (`'2015-13-01'` gives 2016-01-01, moment gives an invalid date), it does not skip text before the date, and two-digit years follow intl. moment parses in forgiving mode: a value that ends before the format ends is valid. The `tryParse` of intl gives `null` for that value. Thus with the parser `'YYYY-MM'` of `controller.bar/bar-thickness-no-overlap.json`, the port parses only `'2017-08'`, and 4 of the 5 x values are NaN, where Chart.js at the pin parses all 5. The ICU twin of this fixture pins the port result through `_unparsed` and `_differs` of `test/port/fixtures/moment_twins_test.dart`. Absent year, month and day fields come from the current local date, also when the default zone is UTC, as in moment. A format that is not a string throws a `TypeError`. moment keys its cache of format functions by the text of the format: after a call with `'YYYY'`, the format `['YYYY']` gives the year, and before that call it throws a `TypeError`. A spec case that asserts moment-token text, parses a moment format string or asserts the moment `FORMATS` strings skips with this row. Its port-only twin with ICU patterns keeps its assertions. | — | `test/port/core/date_adapter_test.dart`, group `adapt-date-moment-format`; `test/port/core/date_adapter_vm_test.dart`, group `current-date fill`; `test/port/scales/scale.time_test.dart`, group `adapt-date-moment-format` (the port-only twins); `test/port/fixtures/moment_twins_test.dart`, group `adapt-date-moment-format` (the ICU twins of the 36 pure data fixtures that skip with this row, tag `fixtures`) | Compare `FORMATS` of the locked chartjs-adapter-moment and the moment-token cases of `scale.time.tests.js` on each bump. Run the group after each `package:intl` upgrade. |
| <a id="adapt-date-parse-fallback"></a>`adapt-date-parse-fallback` | `lib/src/core/date_parse.dart` `dateInput` (the List and Map branch), `parseDateString` (the end); `lib/src/core/date_adapter.dart` `_add` (the amount) | `src/scales/scale.time.js`@v4.6.1:59-64; `karma.conf.cjs`@v4.6.1:106 | adapt | moment parses a string that is not ASP.NET, ISO 8601 or RFC 2822 with `new Date(string)` (`moment.js:2550-2560`). That result depends on the engine and on the zone of the process, and moment marks the form as deprecated. moment also reads an array and an object as date fields, an empty one as the current time, and a duration string (`'P1D'`) or a duration object (`{days: 2}`) as an amount of `add`. The port gives an invalid date (`parse` returns `null`) for such a string, a `List` and a `Map`, and the amount 0 for a duration string or object. `time.md:17` lets the adapter define its input. | — | `test/port/core/date_adapter_test.dart`, group `adapt-date-parse-fallback` | Compare the parse calls of `scale.time.js` and the inputs of the time-scale specs on each bump. |
| <a id="platform-date-local-offset"></a>`platform-date-local-offset` | `lib/src/core/date_zone.dart` `LocalZone` (the platform read); `lib/src/core/date_format.dart` `formatDate` (the `DateTime` range) | `src/scales/scale.time.js`@v4.6.1:62-73, 467-479; `test/index.js`@v4.6.1:23 | platform | In local time, the default members take the offsets of the platform (`DateTime`): on the VM `timeZoneOffset`, which is the offset of the local fields there, and on the web the local fields ([Chart.js ADR 0110](adr/0110-local-zone-offset-cache.md)). The VM and dart2wasm take the offset from a substitute year before 1901-12-13T20:45:53Z and after 2038-01-19T03:14:07Z (beyond ±(2^31 - 1) s). After 2038 the offset is wrong by one hour in zones whose rules changed after the substitute year, for example Moscow, Mexico City, Sao Paulo and Tehran. dart2wasm gives the local mean time offsets before standard time in whole minutes, truncated toward zero (up to 58 s off, in Yakutsk until 1919). dart2js follows JS `Date`. On the VM, the tz data of the operating system can also differ from the tz data of the JS engine: macOS tzdata 2026c gives +00:00 for Africa/Casablanca on 2027-01-01, Node.js v24.12.0 (tz 2025b) gives +01:00. Thus local ticks and parsed values can differ from upstream for these dates. A local wall time outside the range of `DateTime` (the last hours at ±8.64e15 ms) formats with an ICU pattern in UTC. UTC gives the upstream results on every target, as the specs use. | — | `test/port/core/date_zone_test.dart`, group `LocalZone` (the platform values, and `timeZoneOffset` against the local fields on the VM) | None for Chart.js. Re-check on each Flutter upgrade (`.fvmrc`). |
| <a id="platform-date-zone-name"></a>`platform-date-zone-name` | `lib/src/js/js_number.dart` `_zoneName` (the VM branch) | `src/core/core.scale.js`@v4.6.1:819-848; `src/helpers/helpers.canvas.ts`@v4.6.1:46-48 | platform | In V8, `String(date)` ends with the long ICU name of the zone, for example `' (Coordinated Universal Time)'`. `jsToString` of a `DateTime` adds the name that the platform gives (Chart.js ADR 0036). dart2js and dart2wasm read the name from the `Date.prototype.toString` of the engine (`DateTime.timeZoneName`). The VM gives only the abbreviation of the operating system (`'UTC'`, `'GMT'`, `'CET'`) or the name of the Windows zone, and an abbreviation does not give the ICU name: `'GMT'` in Europe/London is `'Greenwich Mean Time'` in 2024 and `'British Summer Time'` in 1906 in V8. Thus on the VM, only UTC gives the name. In another zone, the text ends after the offset. The text is behavior: a `Date` label of a category scale is measured through its `ToString` (`_computeLabelSizes` and `_measureText`), so the name changes the label widths, the label rotation, the scale size and the chart area. In UTC, a bar chart of 800 x 300 with the labels `new Date(0)`, `new Date(86400000)` and `new Date(172800000)` gives `chartArea.left` 282.6, `x.labelRotation` 6.71 and `x.height` 70.71. Without the name, it gives 30.68, 0 and 28.4. Thus in another zone on the VM, the `Date` labels of a category scale are narrower than upstream. UTC gives the upstream results on every target, and the specs use UTC. | — | `test/port/js/js_number_chart_test.dart`, group `platform-date-zone-name`; `test/port/js/js_number_test.dart`, group `jsToString a DateTime` | None for Chart.js. Re-check on each Flutter upgrade (`.fvmrc`). |
| <a id="adapt-layout-configure-types"></a>`adapt-layout-configure-types` | `lib/src/core/core.layouts.dart` `_configure` (`layouts.configure`); `lib/src/types/layout.dart` `LayoutItem.fullSize` | `src/core/core.layouts.js`@v4.6.1:331-332; `src/types/layout.d.ts`@v4.6.1:17 | adapt | layout.d.ts types `fullSize` as a boolean. The port gives the member this type. Thus `configure` stores the truthiness of the `fullSize` option. Upstream stores the option value as it is. An absent `fullSize` gives `false`, where upstream gives `undefined`. Only `core.layouts.js` reads the member, and it tests only its truthiness (:75, :87, :201, :232, :246). Thus the layout of the boxes does not change. `weight` is not part of this row: `LayoutItem.weight` is an `Object?`, and `configure` stores the raw value, because `sortByWeight` compares the raw values with `===`. | — | `test/port/core/core.layouts_test.dart`, group `adapt-layout-configure-types`, and the scene `configure defaults` | Compare `configure`, `addBox` and the readers of `fullSize` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-scale-option-types"></a>`adapt-scale-option-types` | `lib/src/core/core.scale.dart` `Scale.init`, `Scale.getLabels`, `Scale.fit`, `Scale._computeLabelItems` | `src/core/core.scale.js`@v4.6.1:259, :358-361, :649-652, :682-684, :1210, :1322; `src/core/core.layouts.js`@v4.6.1:209-216, :228, :239, :242, :253; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:1317, :3286 | adapt | The `axis` option holds an axis class (row `adapt-axis-objects`): `determineAxis` reads only a `ScaleAxis`, and `init` takes it. `getLabels` returns a `List` (the JSDoc `string[]`), and casts the first truthy value to `List`. A truthy `labels`, `xLabels` or `yLabels` that is not a `List` throws a `TypeError` at `getLabels`. Upstream returns the value. index.d.ts:3286 types the option as `'x' \| 'y' \| 'r'`, and Chart.js never passes another value. The fields `width` and `height` and the `translation` of the label items are `double`. A string option in a `+` (`jsAdd`) can make upstream keep a joined string there: `grid.tickLength: '8'` with hidden tick labels gives the `height` `'80'` (:649, :682), and `ticks.labelOffset: '5'` gives the translation `'405'` (:1210, :1322). The port keeps the `ToNumber` of the string. The readers in core.scale.js and the canvas call `translate` use the same number. One reader does not apply `ToNumber`: `setBoxDims` (core.layouts.js:209-216). `placeBoxes` gives it the size of a box without the `stack` option (:228, :242), and `setBoxDims` adds the size to the left or top edge: a string size joins the edge. A bottom axis at 432 gets the bottom `'43280'`, where the port gives 512. The next box on the same side starts at that edge (:239, :253). Thus the draws move. A second bottom axis gets the top `'403.5999999999999780'` and draws its tick marks and labels at 403.6, where the port gives 483.6. A left axis after another left axis gets the right `'30.68164062580'` and draws its tick marks from 22.68 to 30, where the port gives 102.68 to 110. A bottom edge that is not a number (`'10.2822.4'`, with a title) puts the title at NaN, and the canvas does not draw it. The port draws the title at 821.4, outside the canvas of 512 px. The chart area does not change. A valid configuration gives the same results. | — | `test/port/core/core.scale_test.dart`, group `adapt-scale-option-types`, and the reference cases with string options (`tool/reference/scale_reference.mjs`) | Compare `init`, `getLabels`, `fit` and `_computeLabelItems`, and `setBoxDims` and `placeBoxes` of core.layouts.js, on each bump. Decide the types again if upstream reads another `axis` value. |
| <a id="js-semantics-narrow-labels"></a>`js-semantics-narrow-labels` | `lib/src/scales/scale.category.dart` `_addIfString` | `src/scales/scale.category.js`@v4.6.1:4-12; `src/core/core.scale.js`@v4.6.1:358-361 | js-semantics | Upstream pushes a string that is not a label into the labels array (`labels.push(raw)`, :6). The labels are the live array of `options.labels`, `data.xLabels`, `data.yLabels` or `data.labels` (`getLabels`). The port adds the string to the `List` of the configuration. A `List` takes only the values that its element type allows. A list literal can have a narrow inferred type, for example `List<int>` for `[2020, 2021]` or `List<List<String>>` for multi-line labels. A string that is not a label then throws a `TypeError` at the add: a data value such as `'2021'`, or the option `min: 'x'` (`Scale.init` parses it). JavaScript pushes the string. A `List` that cannot grow throws an `UnsupportedError` at the add of such a string, also with the element type `Object?`. Examples are a `const` literal, a `List.unmodifiable` and a `List.filled`. A JavaScript array literal can always grow. A growable `List<String>` takes the string, and a growable list of the runtime type `List<Object?>` takes every value. Thus labels of these types give the same results as upstream. | — | `test/port/scales/scale.category_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-narrow-labels`, and the reference case `numeric string data on numeric labels` | Compare the writes into the labels array (`addIfString`, `init`) on each bump. |
| <a id="js-semantics-narrow-datasets"></a>`js-semantics-narrow-datasets` | `lib/src/plugins/plugin.colors.dart` `_colorizeDefaultDataset`, `_colorizeDoughnutDataset`, `_colorizePolarAreaDataset`. `lib/src/plugins/plugin.decimation.dart` `_syncDataAccessor`, `_cleanDecimatedDataset`, the first-time block and the `_decimated` write of `_beforeElementsUpdate` | `src/plugins/plugin.colors.ts`@v4.6.1:36-37, 43, 49, `src/plugins/plugin.decimation.js`@v4.6.1:156-168, 253-264, 280 | js-semantics | The colors plugin writes `borderColor` and `backgroundColor` into each dataset object of `chart.config.data.datasets` when it colorizes. The port writes into the Map of the configuration. A Map takes only the values that its value type allows. A Map literal can have a narrow inferred type, for example `Map<String, List<int>>` for `{'data': [1, 2, 3]}`. The write of a color String (the default colorizer) or of a `List<Object?>` of colors (the doughnut and polar area colorizers) then throws a `TypeError`. An unmodifiable Map throws an `UnsupportedError`. JavaScript sets the properties in both cases. The throw comes only when the plugin colorizes: `enabled` is true (the upstream default), and either `forceOverride` is true or no dataset, root or `elements` color is set and `defaults.borderColor` and `defaults.backgroundColor` are unchanged (`plugin.colors.ts:90-121`). The decimation plugin writes `_data`, `data` and `_decimated` into a dataset Map and removes keys from it (`plugin.decimation.js:156-168`, `:253-264`, `:280`). A narrow Map throws a `TypeError` there, for example at the write of the `undefined` getter value into a `Map<String, List<Map<String, int>>>`, and an unmodifiable Map throws an `UnsupportedError`. Without the colorize and the decimation, the same chart works. A growable dataset Map of the runtime type `Map<String, Object?>` takes the writes and gives the upstream results. | — | `test/port/plugins/plugin.colors_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-narrow-datasets`; `test/port/plugins/plugin.decimation_test.dart`, group `js-semantics-narrow-datasets` | Compare the writes into the dataset of the three colorizers (`plugin.colors.ts:35-52`) and of `plugin.decimation.js:156-168`, `:253-264` and `:280` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-decimation-data-accessor"></a>`adapt-decimation-data-accessor` | `lib/src/plugins/plugin.decimation.dart` `_DataAccessor`, `_dataAccessor`, `_syncDataAccessor`, `_cleanDecimatedDataset`, and in `_beforeElementsUpdate` (the hook `beforeElementsUpdate` of `decimation`) the sync call, the first-time block and the `data` write after the `_decimated` write | `src/plugins/plugin.decimation.js`@v4.6.1:156-168, 205-282 | adapt | Upstream replaces the own property `data` of the dataset with an accessor (`:249-265`). The getter gives `_decimated`, and the setter writes `_data`. A Dart `Map` has no accessor for one key ([Chart.js ADR 0038](adr/0038-plugin-modules-class-values-and-interfaces.md) §8). The port keeps `data` as a plain entry that holds the getter value. An `Expando` marks the datasets that have the accessor, and it holds the last value that the plugin wrote into `data`. At the start of the callback of each dataset of `beforeElementsUpdate` and of `cleanDecimatedDataset`, a `data` value that is not identical to that last value counts as a setter write: it goes to `_data`. Then `data` gets the getter value. Thus a user write or delete of `_decimated` changes `data` and not `_data`, as upstream. An absent key `data` (a `delete dataset.data` of the user, which removes the accessor upstream) clears the mark. After `:280`, the port writes `data` only for a marked dataset: a dataset with its own `_data` key before the first decimation has no accessor upstream, and its `data` stays. The writes keep the upstream key order. After each hook, the keys, the arrays and the throws are the upstream ones, also after an early return of `:220-238` and after the `Error` of `:277`. Four results differ. (1) Between a write of `data` and the next `beforeElementsUpdate`, a read of `data` gives the written array, and `_data` is the old raw data. Upstream gives the getter value, and `_data` is the written array. The write can come from the user or from `_dataCheck` (`core.datasetController.js:367`), which writes a new empty array when the getter gives `undefined` (after a user delete of `_decimated` while the plugin cleans nothing). In the same way, after a user write or delete of `_decimated`, a read of `data` gives the old decimated array until the next hook. Upstream gives the new value of `_decimated`. A tooltip in that time reads its `raw` value from `data` (`plugin.tooltip.js:136`). In the same `update()`, the `_dataCheck` of a new controller (`core.datasetController.js:397`) runs before `beforeElementsUpdate` (`core.controller.js:497`) and links the written array. The next `_dataCheck` (`core.datasetController.js:409`) links the same array as upstream. (2) A write of the current decimated array itself (`dataset.data = dataset.data`) is not a change of the Map. Upstream puts the decimated array into `_data`, and the next update decimates the decimated items. The port decimates the old raw data. (3) While no hook of the plugin runs (`plugins: {decimation: false}`), a user write shows the written data at the next update. Upstream keeps the accessor, and the chart keeps the old decimated data. (4) A `delete dataset.data` and a new write of `data` before the next hook are one setter write for the sync, because the key `data` is present. Upstream, the delete removes the accessor and the write makes a plain property: the chart shows the written array without decimation, and `_data` stays the old raw data. The port puts the written array into `_data` and decimates it. A theme leaves `data`, `_data` and `_decimated` unchanged. | — | `test/port/plugins/plugin.decimation_test.dart`, group `adapt-decimation-data-accessor` | Compare `plugin.decimation.js:156-168` and `:205-282`, and each reader and writer of `dataset.data` (`core.datasetController.js` `_dataCheck`, `plugin.tooltip.js` `createTooltipItem`). |
| <a id="js-semantics-decimation-data"></a>`js-semantics-decimation-data` | `lib/src/plugins/plugin.decimation.dart` `_getStartAndCountOfVisiblePointsSimplified` | `src/plugins/plugin.decimation.js`@v4.6.1:12-16, 50, 176-195, 241-245, 269-278 | js-semantics | The plugin reads the data of a decimated dataset (`_data`, else `data`) as an array: `points.length` (`:177`), `_lookupByKey` (`:186`, `:189`), and the item reads of the two algorithms. The port casts the data to a `List` at the start of `getStartAndCountOfVisiblePointsSimplified` (`:241`). Data that is not a `List` throws a `TypeError` there, before the first-time block of `:249-265`. Upstream, `null` and `undefined` throw the same `TypeError` at `:177`. A string or an object goes on: `length` is a number for a string and `undefined` (NaN) for an object. For a string whose length is at or below the threshold, `count <= threshold` (`:243`) is true: upstream calls `cleanDecimatedDataset` (`:245`) and returns. The chart is then the chart without the plugin (x64 Node.js v24.12.0 at the pin: a string of 10 characters gives 10 point elements, as with `enabled: false`). The port throws the `TypeError` for this string too. For an object (`count` is NaN) and for a string above the threshold, upstream defines the accessor of `:249-265`, and (x64 Node.js v24.12.0 at the pin) `min-max` decimates a string of 50 characters to an array of one character. `lttb` reads `samples` as `options.samples || availableWidth` (`:12`). For a string, it gives the string itself when `samples` is not below its length (the `slice` of `:14-16`; the chart then has one element for each character), and an array of the first and the last character when `samples` is 2 or less. When `samples` is 3, it gives an array with an `undefined` item, and `getMinMax` then throws a `TypeError` (`core.datasetController.js:633-636`). When `samples` is from 4 to the length minus 1, it throws a `TypeError` at `:50`. Both algorithms throw a `TypeError` for an object. With `samples` of 2 or less, `lttb` gives `[undefined, undefined]` for an object, and the line controller then throws a `TypeError` at its read of the items. Thus an object throws upstream too, after the first-time block. Such data breaks the documented input of the plugin (`parsing: false` data in the internal format, an array of objects). Thus the port keeps the one cast and does not copy the string and object paths. | — | — (a probe on the pinned build in x64 Node.js v24.12.0 gave the upstream results above) | Compare `plugin.decimation.js:176-195` and the reads of `data` in `:3-154` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-linearbase-tick-bounds"></a>`adapt-linearbase-tick-bounds` | `lib/src/scales/scale.linearbase.dart` `_generateTicks` | `src/scales/scale.linearbase.js`@v4.6.1:111, :136-138, :258-259; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:1195, :3291, :3296 | adapt | `buildTicks` gives the raw `min` and `max` options to `generateTicks` (:258-259). Upstream pushes the raw values as the values of the bound ticks (:111, :138), or writes the raw `max` into the last tick (:136). index.d.ts:1195 types `Tick.value` as a number, and the port keeps a `double` ([Chart.js ADR 0025](adr/0025-scale-value-parameters.md), Open 6). Thus the port writes the `ToNumber` of the option. A number option gives the same results. A string bound, for example `min: '-15'`, gives the tick value `-15`, where upstream gives the string `'-15'`. The numeric formatter then gives another label: upstream compares the string with its floor (`core.ticks.js:91`), and the delta becomes 0. Thus upstream gives the label `'-15.00000000000000000000'`, and the port gives `'-15'`. The other lines of a string bound stay as upstream: `!==` and `===` compare the raw options, and in the cases 1 and 2 `niceMin` and `niceMax` hold the raw options. `_decimalPlaces` of a string is `undefined` (:102), so the ticks between the bounds are NaN, as upstream gives. index.d.ts:3291 and :3296 type the `min` and `max` options of a cartesian scale as numbers. | — | `test/port/scales/scale.linearbase_test.dart`, group `adapt-linearbase-tick-bounds` | Compare `generateTicks` and `buildTicks` on each bump. Remove the row if upstream gives numbers to the bound ticks. |
| <a id="adapt-time-generate-no-advance"></a>`adapt-time-generate-no-advance` | `lib/src/scales/scale.time.dart` `TimeScale.generate` | `src/scales/scale.time.js`@v4.6.1:473-481 | adapt | Upstream repeats `time = +adapter.add(time, stepSize, minor)` while `time < max` (:479). When `add` returns its input, the loop does not end: with chartjs-adapter-moment, `ticks.stepSize` 0.4 with `day` or `month`, 0.5 with `millisecond`, NaN, and 0 for a range below one unit. The browser then hangs. In the port the loop stops the UI isolate and the `flutter test` process. The port throws a `StateError` when the next value is at most `time` (`next <= time`, false for NaN). A NaN value ends the loop, as upstream. Thus the stop changes no result that upstream returns. Upstream guards the same freeze at `:473-476` ([Chart.js ADR 0024](adr/0024-dates.md) §4). | — | `test/port/scales/scale.time_test.dart`, group `adapt-time-generate-no-advance`, and the reference case `an adapter add that gives NaN` | Compare `_generate` on each bump. |
| <a id="adapt-time-null-timestamp"></a>`adapt-time-null-timestamp` | `lib/src/scales/scale.time.dart` `_ticksFromTimestamps`, `_tickValue`, `TimeScale.buildTicks`, `TimeScale.getPixelForTick` | `src/scales/scale.time.js`@v4.6.1:66-68, :156-191, :375-376, :405, :549-556, :661; `src/core/core.scale.js`@v4.6.1:906-912, :1457; `src/types/index.d.ts`@v4.6.1:1195 | adapt | `parse` gives `null` for a label that the adapter cannot parse (:66-68), and `getLabelTimestamps` keeps it (:661). The port keeps it as Dart `null` in the label timestamps. These readers read it as upstream does: the sort, the unique filter, the label bounds, `_filterBetween`, the offsets and the lookup table of the timeseries scale. Each one reads it as 0 in the arithmetic and in the compares. index.d.ts:1195 types `Tick.value` as a number, and the port keeps `Tick.value`, `Scale.min` and `Scale.max` as `double`. With `ticks.source: 'labels'`, upstream gives such a tick the value `null` (:185-191). The port gives the tick the value NaN and marks the tick (`_nullValueTicks`). The readers in `scale.time.dart` read the value of a marked tick as `null` (`_tickValue`). These readers are the major ticks (:156-169), the tick callback and the tick label (:549-556), `+tick.value` of `offsetAfterAutoskip` (:405) and `getPixelForTick` (core.scale.js:906-912). `TimeScale` has a port-only override of `getPixelForTick` for this. Thus the labels, the offsets, the paddings and the pixels are the upstream values. On a time scale, the pixel of that tick is NaN. On a timeseries scale, `getDecimalForValue` reads `null` as 0, and the pixel of that tick is finite. Other code that reads `Tick.value` gets NaN in place of `null`: the scriptable context of the tick (`context.tick`), `getLineWidthForValue` (core.scale.js:1457) and plugins. Thus `getLineWidthForValue(null)` finds no tick, where upstream finds that tick. With `bounds: 'ticks'` and a `null` first or last timestamp, upstream sets `min` or `max` to `null` (:375-376). The port writes 0, the `ToNumber` of `null`. The arithmetic of each reader gives the same result. The label of the capacity (:610-622) and `diff` (:114) get 0 in place of `null`: upstream formats `'Invalid date'`, and moment gives NaN for `diff` with an invalid date (0 for `month`, `quarter` and `year`). Thus the unit can differ. Labels that all parse give the same results. | — | `test/port/scales/scale.time_test.dart`, group `adapt-time-null-timestamp`, and the reference cases `a label that does not parse`, `ticks source labels and a label that does not parse` and `offsetAfterAutoskip and a label that does not parse`; `test/port/scales/scale.timeseries_test.dart`, group `adapt-time-null-timestamp`, and the reference cases `a label that does not parse`, `ticks source labels and a label that does not parse` and `ticks source labels, {x, y} data and a label that does not parse` | Compare `parse`, `getLabelTimestamps`, `buildTicks`, `ticksFromTimestamps` and each read of `tick.value` in `scale.time.js` on each bump. Compare `getPixelForTick` of `core.scale.js` on each bump. |
